**Assistant — Turn 26**

The Haruko batch is immediately different in texture from Rei’s. Rei’s transcripts often ask, “How does a disciplined girl learn softness?” Haruko’s ask almost the inverse:

> **How does a soft, supportive, self-deprecating idol learn that her softness is not secondary to brilliance, but a form of strength that can lead others?**

Across these six files, Haruko is not merely “Sunny Peace’s older-sister type.” She is the character who most clearly embodies **continuity, care, endurance, and inherited encouragement**. She is funny, self-deprecating, occasionally ridiculous, very adult-coded despite her “seventeen” joke, and quietly central to the moral architecture of Hoshimi Pro.

## 1\. 養成所の先生 — Haruko as “strong and kind” senpai

This is the most structurally important file in the batch. It gives Haruko’s mature idol thesis almost directly.

The story begins by emphasizing how other people perceive Haruko: comforting, easy to talk to, reliable, “motherly,” and unusually settled. A nervous junior idol immediately clings to her and wants to call her “お姉様.” Staff and co-stars consult her. People even tell her she may be more suited to backstage support than idol work. Haruko is ambivalent about this because she wants to be seen as a sparkling idol, not merely a soothing presence or “mom friend.” But the story slowly reframes that very quality as her central power. card\_hrk\_001\_st-card-hrk-05-adl…

The BanPro training-school request then forces her to confront her wound. Haruko once auditioned for BanPro’s elite training program and failed in the first round. In her mind, BanPro represents “chosen talent,” the glittering path she was not allowed onto. So being asked to lecture there feels almost absurd: how can the girl once deemed unfit teach the chosen girls?

That insecurity is crucial. Haruko does not see her history of failure as authority. She sees it as disqualification. Makino reframes it: precisely because she endured empty handshake lines, ignored festival stages, losing streaks, bad weather, and obscurity, she has something to teach that dazzling prodigies may need most. Haruko’s expertise is not “how to be naturally chosen.” It is **how to keep going when you are not chosen.**

The Saegusa flashback crystallizes the thesis:

> **他者を想う優しさ、自分を諦めない強さ**
> kindness toward others, and the strength not to give up on oneself.

That is Haruko’s core. She is not “soft instead of strong.” Her softness is inseparable from endurance. She becomes a model for juniors because she knows that idols are not sustained by talent alone, but by a heart that does not break when talent fails to immediately become recognition.

The lecture itself is excellent. Haruko tells the trainees they will suffer, envy others, want to quit, and face days when belief in themselves is difficult. Then she gives them the words she inherited: do not lose, do not break, do not give up. She also tells them the people around them are not enemies but companions chasing the same dream. This line is especially important because we have already seen Rei internalize it from Haruko. Haruko is not only a receiver of wisdom; she is now a transmitter.

The final live, where Haruko invites the trainees and resolves to show them “the back of a senior walking ahead,” completes the arc. She becomes what she once needed: a figure whose very existence says, “You can suffer, fail, remain kind, and still become someone’s dream.”

**Contribution to Haruko:** Core. This is probably one of Haruko’s most important character files. It defines her not as “the gentle one,” but as the idol who turns failure into teachable endurance.

## 2\. 星見プロのお姉さん枠 — Haruko as social bridge among adult-coded women

This file is lighter, but it is very useful for Haruko’s social function. She notices that she, Rio, and miho occupy the “older sister” / adult-coded space in Hoshimi Pro, but do not actually talk that much. So she uses her birthday project as a pretext to drag them into a camping trip. card\_hrk\_002\_st-card-hrk-05-bir…

That premise says a lot about her. Haruko is a connector. She sees underdeveloped relationship lines inside the agency and tries to turn work into community. miho refuses, Rio is uncertain, and Haruko keeps pushing not because she is manipulative in a hard sense, but because she genuinely believes shared experience can make people more open. “Nature makes people open up” is very Haruko: naïve, warm, and not entirely wrong.

The “older sister” discussion is also important. Haruko worries about distance from younger members: she wants dignity, but also closeness; she wants to be approachable, but also a proper senpai. Rio and miho do not consciously theorize this the same way. Rio is more naturally commanding, miho more guarded and pragmatic. Haruko is the one who turns “how do we relate to juniors?” into an explicit emotional question.

The camping chaos — bad route, bugs, tent fire, alcohol, miho’s drunken personality, Rio ending up as the sane one — makes Haruko look ridiculous, but not foolish in a flat way. She creates disorder because she wants connection so badly that she overestimates the power of vibes, nature, and her own camping competence. Yet by the end, Rio admits it was fun, miho admits the camping itself was not bad, and Haruko is already imagining the next trip.

So the file’s real thesis is:

> **Haruko’s relational optimism is sometimes logistically disastrous, but emotionally productive.**

She does not win miho and Rio over through charisma in the conventional idol sense. She wears them down through sincerity, food, alcohol, shared inconvenience, and her refusal to let awkwardness remain permanent.

**Contribution to Haruko:** Important. It develops Haruko as the agency’s social glue, especially among older/professional women who are not naturally easy to gather.

## 3\. 慰安旅行 in山梨 — Haruko’s adult intimacy with Makino and her “let me be cared for” side

This file is very revealing because it gives Haruko a relaxed, adult, lightly romantic-coded dynamic with Makino that differs sharply from Rei’s flustered tsundere embarrassment.

The opening ensemble establishes Haruko’s travel personality: she is excitable, outdoorsy, food-and-drink motivated, and comically committed to the idea that nature refreshes people. She talks about mountain climbing, good food, wine, local scenery, and “living vividly” in a way that makes her feel both older and more childlike at once. card\_hrk\_003\_st-card-hrk-05-bir…

When she approaches Makino privately, her stated reason is practical — she wants help carrying ingredients and equipment for a mountaintop barbecue — but she also admits she wants to talk with him because they have not had much time together lately. This matters. Haruko and Makino have a long history, and she is comfortable wanting his company. Unlike Rei, she does not need to disguise it as a crisis or scolding. She can say, essentially, “I want to spend time with you.”

The shopping sequence shows Haruko’s sensual joy. She gets excited over meat, fish, vegetables, potatoes, cheese, fruit, sake, and Yamanashi wine. This is Haruko as a pleasure-positive character: food, drink, scenery, and companionship are not incidental; they are how she experiences life as restorative.

The mountain scene is even better. Haruko overestimates herself, gets exhausted, and Makino takes her luggage. She calls him kind and cool. At the summit, they share food and wine, reminisce about the old days, and she says he has become adult, strong, and dependable. He says she has become more confident and even cuter. She gets embarrassed, but warmly, not defensively.

Then she rests her head on his shoulder and falls asleep. The emotional function is clear:

> **Haruko is usually the person others lean on; this file lets her lean on someone else.**

That is important because Haruko’s “motherly” role can flatten her if she is only support. This story reminds us that she also wants to be seen, indulged, accompanied, and protected. She is not only a caretaker; she is someone who needs care too.

**Contribution to Haruko:** Very important. It deepens her as an adult-coded woman with history, longing, exhaustion, and a softer private bond with Makino.

## 4\. 工具セットどうするのよ — Haruko’s comic self-image: seventeen forever, food anxiety, and aging denial

This is mostly comic, but it adds valuable texture to Haruko’s self-presentation.

The first section uses Rei’s morning-disaster mode as the setup, but Haruko is the comic interpreter. Rei tries to give her what she thinks is a vegetable set for pickles, but it is actually a tool set, and Haruko has to tsukkomi the entire hallucinated exchange. This also reinforces Haruko’s everyday caretaker position: even on her own birthday morning, she is managing someone else’s absurdity. card\_hrk\_004\_st-card-hrk-05-bir…

The second section focuses on Haruko’s body-image anxiety. After New Year’s/forgetting restraint, a costume feels tight around the stomach, and she becomes reluctant to eat at her own party. Aoi misunderstands the “big” issue as bust-related rather than stomach-related, which sends Haruko into a mistaken chain of reassurance. Haruko then decides it is a “cheat birthday” and will eat to excess. This is funny, but it fits her broader character: she is warm and self-accepting toward others, but not immune to insecurity about age, body, or presentation.

The Sumire/けん玉 scene is even more revealing. Haruko sees Sumire’s innocent smile and experiences it as “true youth.” Rather than simply envy it, she decides to learn from it. This is Haruko’s ridiculous aspiration: to become a “perfect seventeen-year-old.” The result is a surreal kendama training sequence where she tries to become “the kendama itself.”

This joke lands because Haruko’s “seventeen” insistence is not just vanity. It is a defense against being categorized only as older, motherly, settled, or past her prime. She wants freshness too. She wants to be silly, youthful, sparkling, and a little absurd. She knows people see her as an older-sister/motherly figure, and part of her wants to resist being sealed into that role.

**Contribution to Haruko:** Nice-to-important texture. It does not redefine her, but it clarifies her insecurity around age, freshness, body image, and being perceived as too “settled.”

## 5\. 羞恥心克服 — Haruko’s femininity, visibility, and the labor behind “being looked at”

This is one of the batch’s most useful files because it examines Haruko’s relationship to sensual visibility.

At first, Haruko is excited by a new costume because she hopes it will break her “plain” image. But when she sees the outfit, she freezes. It exposes more skin than expected, emphasizes her body line, thighs, and arms, and she becomes especially embarrassed at the thought of Makino seeing her in it. That specificity is important. She is not merely embarrassed by the audience; the first emotional blockage is being seen by someone familiar. card\_hrk\_005\_st-card-hrk-05-chn…

The story then becomes absurd “shame-overcoming training”: wearing the outfit around the dorm, taking Shizuku-directed gravure photos, getting swept up in posing, then panicking when Makino receives the photos. This is comedy, but it reveals Haruko’s split relationship to her own attractiveness. She wants attention. She wants to stop being “plain.” She wants to be visibly glamorous. But when attention becomes explicitly sensual — “sexy,” body lines, bold poses — she overheats and retreats.

The resolution is not “Haruko becomes comfortable being sexy.” It is more ethically/professionally grounded. Makino redirects her to Satomi, who worked hard on the costume. Haruko realizes the outfit is not merely a source of embarrassment; it is the crystallization of other people’s labor. Designers, stylists, makeup, lighting, staging — many people are trying to help her shine. So wearing the costume becomes an act of gratitude, not just self-display.

That is a very Haruko solution. She can overcome embarrassment when the act is recontextualized as **care returned to the people who cared for her**. She can stand proudly because she is not doing it only for herself.

The live itself keeps the vulnerability intact. The fans call her sexy, she blushes, tries to project adult charm, then collapses back into embarrassment. The punchline is that fans love her precisely because she is both attractive and flustered. But the serious point is:

> **Haruko’s idol visibility is not effortless. She has to choose to be seen, and she chooses it by remembering the people who made her shine.**

**Contribution to Haruko:** Very important. It develops her femininity, professional gratitude, self-image insecurity, and stage courage.

## 6\. 一週間マネージャー — Haruko’s origin wound, Mana, Makino, and the promise fulfilled

This is the strongest overall file in the batch, along with 養成所の先生. It is essential to Haruko’s place in the larger IDOLY PRIDE architecture.

The first two sections are set years earlier, when Makino temporarily manages Haruko while normally being Mana’s manager. This immediately positions Haruko in Mana’s shadow. She jokes about Makino’s politeness, asks him to drop honorific distance, and says he is easy to talk to because they are close in age, shared school context, and less formal than Saegusa. She even says he could continue as her manager, then retreats when he earnestly says he is Mana’s manager. card\_hrk\_006\_st-card-hrk-05-fes…

That small exchange is important. Haruko is not jealous in a melodramatic way, but she is aware of the hierarchy: Mana is the brilliant center, Makino belongs to Mana, and Haruko is the struggling idol getting small jobs. Her self-deprecation is pointed. She thinks her local croquette-eating report work must be disappointing compared to managing a popular idol like Mana. Makino insists she has a different charm: a healing atmosphere, a smile under hardship, thoughtfulness, and steady effort.

This is one of the earliest articulations of Haruko’s identity:

> **Haruko cannot become Mana, but she can become a top idol through Haruko’s own form of reassurance, endurance, and care.**

The Mana promise is the core. Haruko and Mana had planned to one day perform together and deliver gratitude. Haruko is insecure about whether she can stand beside Mana, but Makino encourages her to challenge herself and not fear failure. This becomes a delayed promise whose fulfillment happens only years later, after Mana’s death and after Haruko has become part of Sunny Peace.

The present-day section is extremely important for the whole Hoshimi Pro mythos. Haruko secretly arranges a live for Makino, Saegusa, and Satomi. She performs with the current Hoshimi idols, using a song that includes the feelings of all Hoshimi idols — including Mana. The file explicitly frames the song as connecting Hoshimi Pro’s past and future. Haruko’s role is to carry Mana’s unfinished promise into a living communal form.

This is the Haruko equivalent of the broader IDOLY PRIDE thesis:

> **Haruko is not the idol who replaces Mana. She is the idol who helps Mana’s gratitude continue without trapping Hoshimi in grief.**

Makino’s internal narration confirms it: Haruko has grown through years of unrewarded effort, and now she is indispensable to Hoshimi Pro. That is an enormous statement. Haruko’s importance is not explosive charisma but continuity. She keeps promises alive until the world is ready to receive them.

**Contribution to Haruko:** Core. This file connects Haruko’s personal history, Mana, Makino, gratitude, failure, endurance, and Hoshimi’s inheritance structure.

## Batch synthesis

This Haruko batch gives us a very strong character thesis:

> **Saeki Haruko is the idol of delayed flowering.**

She is not the miracle. She is not the prodigy. She is not the genius dancer or overwhelming star presence. She is the girl who kept going after being told, implicitly and explicitly, that she did not belong among chosen idols. Her power is that she survived obscurity without becoming bitter.

The main domains clarified here are:

**Haruko as failed-but-enduring idol.**
Her past is full of small jobs, ignored performances, BanPro rejection, empty audiences, and comparison to Mana. But none of that turns her cruel. It becomes the foundation of her ability to guide juniors.

**Haruko as transmitter of inherited encouragement.**
The words she received from Hoshimura and Saegusa — kindness toward others, not giving up on oneself, treating others as comrades — become words she passes to Rei and later to BanPro trainees.

**Haruko as Hoshimi’s continuity figure.**
The Mana promise file makes this explicit. Haruko helps turn Mana’s unfinished gratitude into a song belonging to the living agency. This makes her one of the most important “memory mediators” in the cast.

**Haruko as older-sister/motherly figure who resists being reduced to that.**
She is trusted, confided in, and relied upon, but she also wants to be cute, youthful, sexy, glamorous, and sparkling. Her “seventeen” gag is funny because it pushes against everyone treating her as mature before she is ready to relinquish girlish idolhood.

**Haruko as social glue.**
She gathers difficult people: Rio, miho, juniors, trainees, Makino, Mana’s memory, Sunny Peace. She creates settings where people can talk, eat, drink, camp, laugh, and become less alone.

**Haruko as someone who needs care too.**
The Yamanashi trip with Makino is crucial because it lets her be tired, tipsy, affectionate, and sleepy. The caretaker gets to be watched over.

## Priority ranking

**Core:**
一週間マネージャー; 養成所の先生.

**Very important:**
羞恥心克服; 慰安旅行 in山梨.

**Important texture:**
星見プロのお姉さん枠.

**Nice-to-have comedy / self-image texture:**
工具セットどうするのよ.

## Updated Haruko thesis

The cleanest formulation after this batch is:

> **Saeki Haruko is Sunny Peace’s delayed-blooming senior: a “strong and kind” idol who turns years of failure, obscurity, and comparison into warmth that can guide others. Her gift is not overwhelming brilliance but continuity — she preserves promises, mediates grief, comforts juniors, gathers isolated adults, and teaches that the people around you are not enemies but companions chasing the same dream. At the same time, her comedy comes from the fact that this motherly, trusted, adult-coded figure still wants to be seen as youthful, cute, sexy, sparkling, and seventeen. Haruko’s idolhood is the proof that endurance itself can become radiance.**

I’ll go numerically by card ID, because this batch forms a strong developmental sequence: Haruko’s baseline self-image, her post-success insecurity, her unresolved promise with Mana, her Okinawa wound, her relationship to Mana’s birthday and 「voyage」, and finally her romantic/adult-comedy dynamic with Makino and IIIX.

The overall update is:

> **This batch makes Haruko one of the clearest “delayed fulfillment” characters in IDOLY PRIDE.**
> She is repeatedly the girl who did not get the part, did not get the spotlight, did not feel chosen, and did not believe she belonged beside Mana — but years later, through persistence, care, and accumulated trust, she becomes the person who can inherit songs, fulfill promises, support others, and finally say: “I am not here by accident.”

## 1\. ソロキャンが趣味 — Haruko’s baseline: “even my plainness can become useful”

This is an excellent foundational Haruko card because it gives her ordinary-life identity before the later inheritance-heavy stories. The premise is modest: shortly after Tsuki no Tempest and Sunny Peace debut, Haruko gets a local magazine camping job because her solo-camping blog caught an editor’s attention. It is not a grand stage; it is a town-magazine leisure feature. But for Haruko, that is exactly why it matters. Her “plain” hobby becomes professional value. card\_hrk\_007\_st-card-hrk-05-ido…

Haruko’s camping personality is immediately clear: she is nurturing, practical, lightly bossy, food-focused, and delighted by small pleasures. She grills meat and vegetables for Makino, reminds him to eat vegetables, teaches him about marshmallows, and corrects him that it is “solo camp,” not “one-person camping.” The tone is comic, but it shows how care naturally becomes her language. She does not perform “older sister” as a pose; she reflexively feeds, advises, and shares.

The important emotional line is when she says she feels relieved that one of her hobbies could be useful. That reveals the wound under the cheerfulness. Haruko thinks of herself as “地味” — plain, unflashy, not obviously idol-like. Her hobbies are puzzles and camping, not glamorous star material. So when camping becomes work, it feels like evidence that her ordinary self may still have idol value.

The star conversation is also very important. Looking at the night sky, Haruko says only a handful of stars continue shining brightly, like idols. This is classic Haruko self-placement: she knows the industry is severe and that most people do not become first-magnitude stars. Yet because Makino has praised her and recognized her usefulness, she can imagine becoming one of those lights. This is not explosive confidence; it is confidence lent to her by someone else’s belief.

The key contribution is:

> **Haruko’s idolhood begins from the question, “Can someone ordinary and plain still shine?”**

The answer this card gives is yes, but not by becoming Mana. Haruko shines through care, steadiness, local familiarity, hobbies, food, warmth, and the ability to make others feel at ease.

## 2\. 新曲のジャケット撮影 — Haruko after success: imposter syndrome becomes resolve

This is one of the most important Haruko files because it prevents her post-success arc from becoming too clean. Sunny Peace has already risen dramatically after I-UNITY, and Makino asks Haruko to lead the jacket-shooting concept for 「SUNNY PEACE for You and Me！」 because she is the group’s communicator and organizer. On paper, this is recognition. In practice, it triggers fear. card\_hrk\_008\_st-card-hrk-05-ido…

At first Haruko is stunned by the level of staff now working with Sunny Peace: an internationally active photographer, a major creative director, professionals who are not just doing a job but are actually Sunny Peace fans — even Haruko fans. She asks Makino to pinch her because she cannot believe this is real. This scene is comic, but it marks a huge status shift: the former ignored idol is now being taken seriously by elite creators.

But the recognition destabilizes her. During the meeting, she freezes and defers: “hold,” “take back,” “I can’t give opinions to people like this.” That is Haruko’s old inferiority returning in a new form. She is no longer being rejected by the industry; she is being welcomed by it. But because she spent so long as “not chosen,” welcome itself feels suspicious.

The nighttime NEXT VENUS venue scene is the emotional core. Haruko admits that the final stage mattered to her because Mana never stood there, and because she sees herself as the person who watched “idol Nagase Mana” next most closely after Makino. She also admits that even now, she wonders whether she belongs: Sakura has natural radiance, Rei has dance, while Haruko lacks youth, flash, and obvious specialty. Her fear is not simply “I’m not good enough.” It is more specific:

> **“Maybe I am only here because Sunny Peace carried me.”**

Makino’s response is important because he names Haruko’s distinct power: she sends smiles and encouragement even when thinking of others before herself; she has professional experience; she helped guide everyone; her presence made Sunny Peace possible. Haruko then reveals that she never meant to quit — she wanted to hear him praise her a little longer — but the serious decision underneath is real: she will work many times harder, stay with everyone as long as possible, and later upgrades that vow into making Sunny Peace the world’s best idol group. card\_hrk\_008\_st-card-hrk-05-ido…

The card’s contribution is major:

> **Haruko’s insecurity does not vanish when she succeeds. Success forces her to decide whether she will keep treating herself as lucky cargo or accept responsibility as someone who belongs.**

By the end, she does not merely accept being in Sunny Peace; she decides to lead from inside it.

## 3\. ミュージカルのオーディション — Mana as belief, Haruko as delayed co-star

This is a core Haruko/Mana file. It gives one of the clearest explanations of what Mana meant to Haruko personally, not just structurally.

The present-day setup is that Haruko has watched a musical and receives a new audition offer for a revival of the same musical Mana once starred in. That immediately sends her back to the past, when Mana had been cast as the lead and Haruko auditioned for the protagonist’s best friend role. Haruko’s motivation was not primarily love of musicals. It was the chance to stand beside Mana. card\_hrk\_009\_st-card-hrk-05-lin…

That is the important emotional configuration: Haruko is not trying to surpass Mana here. She is trying to reach the place beside her. Because Mana became popular immediately while Haruko remained a struggling idol, ordinary work would never naturally put them on the same stage. The audition looked like a rare opening in the hierarchy. If Haruko won the role, she could be Mana’s partner in a form recognized by the world.

Mana’s response is beautiful because she is not patronizing. She is genuinely happy that Haruko wants to perform with her and admits she would feel reassured having Haruko nearby during her first musical. That reverses Haruko’s inferiority for a moment: Haruko thinks she needs Mana’s stage to become visible, but Mana also wants Haruko beside her as emotional support.

The second section is devastating because Haruko fails. Worse, she had felt the audition went well. She had practiced late, done the readings, believed she had a chance, and then got rejected anyway. On her birthday morning, Mana finds her crying in the office. Haruko apologizes for failing to meet Mana’s expectations and spirals into “I’m no good.” Mana physically lifts her cheeks and tells her to show the smile she loves. This is a very Mana-like rescue: she refuses to let Haruko collapse into self-erasure. She says the experience will not be wasted, that if Haruko does not give up, she will someday stand on the same stage. card\_hrk\_009\_st-card-hrk-05-lin…

The present-day conclusion fulfills that in a complicated way. Haruko has finally passed the audition — not for the friend role, but for the protagonist, the role Mana once played. She imagines Mana appearing beside her, telling her to sing and dance together. Then she acknowledges it is impossible, a future that might have been but is no longer real. Yet she still treats the performance as a fulfilled promise: she is standing on Mana’s stage, with Mana’s words still inside her.

The cleanest formulation:

> **Mana gave Haruko the belief that failure was not the end of the path. Years later, Haruko proves that belief true by standing where Mana once stood — not as Mana’s replacement, but as the friend who never stopped walking toward her.**

This card is core because it makes Haruko’s delayed flowering emotionally concrete.

## 4\. 苦い思い出 — Okinawa, weather, and the humiliation of not being “chosen”

This card deepens Haruko’s “not Mana” wound through a specific memory. Okinawa is not just a location; it is the site of one of Haruko’s most bitter early failures.

Haruko recalls her first Okinawa live: she was nervous, then rain began exactly when her turn came. The audience evacuated, but she had already started singing and could not stop, so she finished the performance soaked, facing an empty crowd. The factual failure is bad enough, but the comparison to Mana makes it unbearable. Haruko remembers the famous Mana episode where a rainy outdoor performance turned into a miraculous success under a cleared sky. Haruko knows logically that Mana cannot control the weather, but emotionally the contrast feels like proof: Mana is “持ってる,” Haruko is not. card\_hrk\_010\_st-card-hrk-05-miz…

That phrase matters. This is not simply a failed gig. It is a metaphysical humiliation: the world itself seemed to cooperate with Mana and reject Haruko. She interprets weather as destiny, bad luck as lack of chosenness. When Makino later encouraged her to try Okinawa again, she accepted because he had unexpectedly noticed and cared about her failure. Then the next year’s event was canceled by a typhoon, reinforcing the wound.

The middle section shifts to body insecurity during the new Okinawa live. Haruko sees Sunny Peace in swimsuit-style costumes and worries that she alone looks “むちっと” compared with the thinner members. The comedy is broad — Makino tries to praise her and accidentally confirms the exact word she fears — but it belongs to the same insecurity complex. Haruko compares herself to others and converts difference into deficiency. Whether the comparison is Mana’s star-luck or other members’ body types, Haruko’s first instinct is: “I am the one who does not fit.”

The successful live resolves the Okinawa trauma. Haruko performs confidently, turns water into excitement rather than disaster, and gives Okinawa a new memory. The night beach scene with Makino is one of her most intimate reflective moments. She explicitly credits him for repeatedly pulling her forward: after her early audition failure, after the musical-role temptation, after the Okinawa wound. She says if he had not been there, she may have given up somewhere along the way. card\_hrk\_010\_st-card-hrk-05-miz…

The ending is lightly romantic-coded but also deeply character-relevant. Makino says she can call him when she needs the sea; Haruko answers that he can take her to the sea even when she has no worries. That is Haruko at her most adult-flirtatious: she is more conscious than Rei, more playful, and less panicked. But the deeper point is trust. The sea is where Haruko processes grief, failure, and self-doubt. Inviting Makino there means inviting him into that vulnerable mental space.

Contribution:

> **This card shows Haruko transforming a place of humiliation into a place of renewal. It also clarifies that Makino is not merely her manager; he is one of the recurring witnesses who kept her from quitting.**

## 5\. そんな光景を見てみたい — Mana’s birthday, Haruko’s pride, and 「voyage」 as personal inheritance

This is a major Mana/Haruko inheritance card, arguably the most important of the batch together with the musical card.

The story begins with Haruko remembering Mana’s birthday and the 2017 birthday live. In the flashback, she helps Makino and Saegusa prepare a surprise for Mana, including a large cake and a penlight heart made by the audience. This is crucial because Haruko chooses backstage support instead of guesting onstage. She helps create the “light” for Mana rather than stand in it herself. card\_hrk\_011\_st-card-hrk-05-mna…

At first, this looks like humility. Later, Haruko reveals it was also pride and insecurity. Mana had invited her to guest, and Haruko was happy, but she refused because she did not feel she could stand on Mana’s important stage and hold her head high. She was chasing Mana, failing, and not yet ready to appear beside her in front of that audience. She resolved that by the next year she would grow enough to celebrate Mana from the stage. But that “next year” never came.

This is one of Haruko’s deepest wounds: not just “I failed,” but “I postponed something important until I was worthy, and time ran out.”

The 「voyage」 revelation is beautiful. Haruko suspects that Mana’s song may have been a present for her — an encouragement song for someone trying to move forward despite anxiety. Makino confirms that Mana rushed the production because there was someone she wanted to reach quickly. Haruko realizes Mana saw her struggle, understood her pride, and answered with a song. card\_hrk\_011\_st-card-hrk-05-mna…

Then Haruko decides to stand on Mana’s birthday stage at Tsuki no Tempest’s live and sing 「voyage」. The performance is a layered act of love: to Mana, to Kotono’s group, to Makino, to the fans, and to everyone who needs encouragement. Haruko frames the song as something that helps listeners take one step toward the future shown by their inner compass. During the performance, she internally says she had been moving toward Mana as a star, trying to become like her, but Mana had told her she was fine as Haruko. That let her find her own compass. card\_hrk\_011\_st-card-hrk-05-mna…

The final revelation — Mana intended 「voyage」 for Haruko to sing too — is one of the strongest inheritance moments in IDOLY PRIDE. Haruko had thought she was borrowing Mana’s song; in fact, Mana had always imagined the song passing to her. Makino asks Haruko to keep singing it, and Haruko accepts it as “Mana’s and my song.”

That is enormous. It means Haruko does not inherit Mana by imitating her miracle. She inherits Mana by carrying forward an encouragement that was addressed to her and through her to others.

Contribution:

> **Haruko becomes one of Mana’s legitimate musical heirs, not because she shares Mana’s voice or aura, but because Mana entrusted her with the work of encouragement.**

## 6\. ドキドキ温泉旅行 — adult comedy, social bridging, and Haruko’s conscious flirtation with Makino

This file is lighter than the Mana-centered cards but very revealing for Haruko’s adult social and romantic texture.

The setup is pure comedy: Haruko has an empty day off and comes to the office because she is lonely, then Makino abruptly asks her to come to a hot-spring inn and stay overnight. Haruko immediately interprets it as potentially two-person romantic travel and gets flustered, then disappointed when he explains it is actually an emergency involving IIIX. card\_hrk\_012\_st-card-hrk-05-one…

That opening is important because Haruko’s romantic-coded response is much more adult and conscious than Rei’s. Rei usually panics after emotional exposure; Haruko often recognizes the situation’s romantic shape and plays with it. She tells Makino to phrase things carefully when inviting other girls, which is both responsible-senpai advice and evidence that she understood the implication immediately.

The IIIX bridging angle also matters. Makino believes Haruko can act as a bridge because she once helped him settle into Hoshimi Pro with tea, kindness, and listening. Haruko is scared of IIIX’s hostility, especially their internal dysfunction, but she tries. That confirms her agency role: Haruko is the person called on when emotionally difficult people need a soft landing. She is Hoshimi’s human de-escalation tool.

The bath fantasy is one of the most overt adult-comedy moments for Haruko. While soaking alone, she imagines washing Makino’s back as a thank-you gesture, realizes what she is imagining, and gets embarrassed. That is not accidental subtext; the story is explicitly playing with Haruko’s adult romantic/sexual imagination, though still in a comedic and non-explicit way. card\_hrk\_012\_st-card-hrk-05-one…

The “be my boyfriend for today” section is even clearer. Haruko openly asks Makino to pretend to be her boyfriend so she can experience the feeling of a couple’s hot-spring trip. She insists on no honorifics and wants him to call her Haruko. When he finally does, catches her as she stumbles, and acts boyfriend-like, the effect is stronger than she expected; she turns red, panics, and ends the game. Unlike Rei, Haruko initiated the roleplay knowingly. The comedy comes from her underestimating her own vulnerability.

The final callback before the live — Makino calling her “Haruko” to relax her — shows that the emotional residue remains. It flusters her again, but also steadies the scene. The name itself becomes charged because it temporarily collapses professional distance.

Contribution:

> **This card confirms Haruko’s Makino dynamic as adult, playful, mutually familiar, and romantic-coded. It also shows that Haruko’s social gift has limits: even she cannot magically fix IIIX sober, so she resorts to food, drink, proximity, and atmosphere.**

## Batch synthesis

This batch substantially deepens Haruko beyond “kind older sister” and even beyond “delayed-blooming idol.” The strongest added thesis is:

> **Haruko is a character whose life is structured by postponed stages.**
> She postponed standing beside Mana until she was worthy. She postponed celebrating Mana onstage until next year. She postponed believing she belonged in Sunny Peace until success forced the question. She postponed seeing her own ordinary hobbies, body, and warmth as idol assets. Her growth is the gradual conversion of postponement into performance.

The most important domains clarified here are:

**Haruko and Mana:**
Mana is not only a shining rival or dead friend. Mana is the person who believed in Haruko when Haruko could not believe in herself. 「voyage」 becomes the key symbol: a song Mana wrote as encouragement, a song Haruko later sings back to Mana, and finally a song Haruko accepts as belonging to both of them.

**Haruko and Makino:**
Makino is one of Haruko’s longest-standing witnesses. He saw her small jobs, her Okinawa humiliation, her musical disappointment, her anxiety around Mana, her post-success impostor syndrome, and her eventual confidence. Their dynamic is adult-coded, familiar, lightly flirtatious, and built on years of shared history. Haruko can tease him, lean on him, drink with him, ask him to play boyfriend, and confess things she would otherwise hide.

**Haruko and Sunny Peace:**
Haruko is not merely lucky to be in Sunny Peace. The later jacket-shoot card explicitly argues the reverse: Sunny Peace was lucky to have her. She brought professional experience, emotional support, group cohesion, and the ability to keep going when recognition was absent.

**Haruko’s self-image:**
She still sees herself as plain, older, not naturally radiant, not as skilled as Rei, not as star-born as Sakura, not as miraculous as Mana. But this batch shows her learning that her exact form of “ordinary” is the thing that makes her necessary.

**Haruko’s idol philosophy:**
An idol is not only someone who shines first and draws others toward them. An idol can also be someone who receives encouragement, survives failure, and then returns that encouragement to the next person. Haruko’s idolhood is reciprocal and transmissive.

## Priority ranking

**Core:**
ミュージカルのオーディション; そんな光景を見てみたい; 新曲のジャケット撮影.

**Very important:**
苦い思い出; ソロキャンが趣味.

**Important / high-texture:**
ドキドキ温泉旅行.

## Updated Haruko thesis

The stronger formulation after this batch:

> **Saeki Haruko is the idol of delayed fulfillment: the girl who was not chosen early, who watched Mana’s back from below, who failed auditions and sang to empty seats, yet kept carrying the belief others placed in her until it became her own. Her gift is not miraculous brilliance but transmissible encouragement. Mana gave her belief; Makino witnessed her persistence; Sunny Peace gave her a place where her care could become group strength. By the time she sings 「voyage」, Haruko is no longer merely chasing Mana’s star. She has become a compass for others — proof that even if the promised stage comes late, the years spent walking toward it were not wasted.**

**Assistant — Turn 30**

This Haruko batch is extremely important because it takes things that were previously implicit — her old awareness of Makino, her anxiety about “youth,” her adult romantic curiosity, her body/self-image issues, her exhaustion, her caretaker identity, and her need to accept BIG4 status — and makes them textually explicit.

The cleanest batch thesis is:

> **Haruko is no longer only the delayed-blooming idol who learned to shine after years of obscurity. She is now a woman trying to reconcile four lives at once: the struggling girl who once chased Mana, the dependable older-sister of Sunny Peace, the BIG4 idol whose influence changes how people treat her, and the ordinary adult woman who wants romance, dates, wine, pajamas, Christmas parties, and someone safe to lean on.**

## 1\. 一緒に頑張ろうって言ったでしょ？ — Haruko as support pillar, and the cost of becoming “big”

This card begins with Rei’s injury and overwork, which immediately places Haruko in her most natural role: the caretaker-senpai who worries, prepares recovery support, and starts thinking about how to fill gaps in the schedule. What matters is not just that she cares about Rei; it is that she reflexively converts concern into logistics. Rei needs rest, therefore Haruko must help cover work, coordinate with Makino, monitor Rei’s tendency to overpractice, and keep Sunny Peace running. card\_hrk\_013\_st-card-hrk-05-pai…

The title line — “一緒に頑張ろうって言ったでしょ？” — is very Haruko. She does not frame support as noble sacrifice. She frames it as shared obligation: we said we would do this together, so I will do my part. This is the same “strong and kind” philosophy from her instructor/mentor stories, now applied to Sunny Peace’s daily operation.

The “むちむち怪獣ハルドン” gag is comic, but useful. Haruko’s body insecurity remains persistent. She takes a miniature-city jacket concept and immediately fears the subtext is that she is a giant, soft-bodied monster. This is absurd, but not random: she is still anxious about being larger, older, softer, and less “idol-like” than her younger members.

The second half is much more serious. As Haruko takes Rei’s substitute work, she is effective and charismatic even when she is not technically “right” for the job. On the quiz program, she forgets answers, gets teased, solves unexpected music questions, and turns mistakes into atmosphere. That is a very important contrast to Rei. Rei’s competence is sharp and technical. Haruko’s competence is ambient and social: she can make a scene work even when she is not winning the quiz.

The old staff member’s formality hurts her. Haruko wants to remain approachable and unchanged, but BIG4 status has altered how others perceive her. The jacket shoot resolves that: the miniature-city concept is not “Haruko as monster,” but Sunny Peace as something that has grown huge without noticing. Haruko realizes that being “big” comes with influence, shadow, distance, and responsibility. She has to accept that people may treat her differently — not because she has become arrogant, but because she has become important.

Contribution:

> **This card shows Haruko accepting stature. She has grown large enough to affect other people’s behavior, and maturity requires not rejecting that out of embarrassment.**

## 2\. 私をデートに連れてって — Haruko’s ideal intimacy: safety, domesticity, and being able to lean

This is one of the most revealing Haruko/Makino cards. The surface premise is a magazine恋愛特集: Haruko needs to talk about ideal dates but has never actually dated. That already says a lot. Haruko is adult-coded, romantic, domestic, warm, and socially intuitive, yet her own romantic life was deferred by idol ambition. She knows care, but not dating. She knows companionship, but not恋愛 as lived experience. card\_hrk\_014\_st-card-hrk-05-paj…

Her first panic is image-related: she fears giving a transparently imaginary answer and being exposed as inexperienced. But the deeper reason she wants to do the job is sincere. She wants to help readers feel encouraged in love, just as she herself has been encouraged through song and performance. She does not want to fake that care.

The date plan falling apart because of sudden rain is symbolically perfect. Haruko tries to study “standard dating” through guides — theme parks, films, popular date spots — but circumstances force her into something more personal: an at-home date involving puzzles, cooking, shared food, old memories of Mana and Makino, a strange romance movie, and eventually Haruko falling asleep. This is not the glossy date she imagined, but it becomes the one that actually teaches her something.

The later interview makes the meaning explicit. Haruko’s ideal partner is not primarily thrilling, flashy, or dramatic. It is someone she can relax with, someone she can lean on, someone who makes her feel cared for. That is almost a direct description of what Makino provides her in the date scenario and across her history. He is not the fantasy prince; he is the person beside her in quiet, domestic, practical, emotionally safe spaces.

The pajama-buying scene adds a playful but important layer. Haruko asks Makino which pajamas he likes because they are her “home date battle outfit.” When the clerk asks if he is her boyfriend, she answers with a pause: “すっごく頼りになる、大事な……マネージャー.” The joke is that “manager” is technically the correct answer, but the pause leaves emotional residue. She then says they should do an at-home date again.

Contribution:

> **This card clarifies that Haruko’s romantic imagination is domestic rather than dramatic. Her ideal intimacy is not being swept away; it is being safe enough to be ordinary.**

## 3\. むちっとはしてるけど — the dorm mother, the tired woman, and the “love expression” she accidentally has

This card is deceptively important because it ties together three Haruko threads: body insecurity, food/care, and romantic softening around Makino.

The opening grocery sequence shows her as Sunny Peace’s practical caretaker. Everyone is busy, most of the group is still in high school, and Haruko deliberately takes on cooking duty to reduce their burden. But she also admits a very funny secondary reason: if she is cooking, she can avoid being asked math questions by the girls doing homework. That is very Haruko — nurturing, but also comic, self-aware, and a little evasive. card\_hrk\_015\_st-card-hrk-05-sai…

The dinner scene makes the family motif explicit. Haruko says that watching the girls study while she cooks makes the dorm feel like a real family. Her cooking is not just utility; it is the ritual that turns idol dorm life into home. This strongly reinforces Sunny Peace’s “mutual warmth” identity. Sakura may be the sun, Rei may be discipline transformed into joy, but Haruko is the table.

Her body anxiety remains active: she worries about being “むちっと,” about cakes, about Rei’s warnings, about whether she can maintain her costume body. But the card does not treat that purely as insecurity. It is linked to idol professionalism and to the ordinary fact that Haruko loves food, cooking, and feeding people. Her body is not abstract; it is where appetite, care, work, femininity, and self-consciousness collide.

The fatigue section is important because it undercuts the “reliable older sister” fantasy. Haruko is overworked. She yawns, leans against Makino, falls asleep, and is embarrassed but refreshed afterward. Again, she is usually the one who supports others, but Makino gives her a place where she can stop holding herself up for a moment.

The final CM shoot makes the romantic subtext much clearer. Haruko asks Makino to “watch me,” then performs well. When asked to make a soft expression like someone in love, she produces it in one take. She later says that the day she spent with Makino probably affected the expression. Makino does not understand, which is exactly the recurring dynamic: Haruko is emotionally literate enough to know what happened; Makino is sincere enough to miss it.

Contribution:

> **This card shows that Haruko’s ability to look “in love” is not acting from nowhere. It emerges from feeling watched, supported, accompanied, and youthful beside Makino.**

## 4\. ちょっと意識していた人 — the text finally says the quiet part

This is one of the most important Haruko/Makino cards because it turns the implied old crush into explicit history.

The first section starts with Haruko’s anxiety about wearing a school-uniform-style outfit beside actual high schoolers. This is not just vanity. She remembers being evaluated as lacking初々しさ even when she was young. Haruko’s age insecurity is really an “I was never young enough in the right way” wound. Even when she was a middle-schooler, the industry made her feel insufficiently fresh. The Valentine costume reopens that wound. card\_hrk\_016\_st-card-hrk-05-vln…

Then she explains that she has basically no恋愛経験 because from middle school onward she was consumed by idol training. This matters for all the date/romance cards: Haruko is not naive because she is childish; she is inexperienced because ambition swallowed the years when ordinary romance would have happened.

The flashback is crucial. When Makino had just joined Hoshimi Pro, Haruko was aware of him. She notices that he knows her schedule despite being Mana’s manager, wonders if he is interested in her, then immediately tells herself not to misunderstand. She describes this later as an admiration for an older boy close in age — but the scene itself is absolutely romantic-coded. She is flustered, hyper-aware, and self-correcting.

Makino’s importance grows because he treats her seriously when she fails auditions. When she cries after another rejection, he does not laugh, pity her, or tell her to be realistic. He says she is already a wonderful idol, that her charm simply has not reached people yet, and offers to practice auditions with her. That becomes the basis for her Valentine chocolate. It is the first chocolate she made for a man other than her father.

She calls it obligation chocolate, but the whole story undermines that. She cannot say Valentine, cannot say chocolate, panics repeatedly, waits until night, and finally gives it to him. His delighted reaction becomes one of the reasons she later enjoys cooking: Makino’s praise teaches her that feeding someone can become emotional communication.

The present-day chocolate-making scene is very flirtatious: he still owes her a return gift years later, fails to make it alone, they make chocolate together, and she licks chocolate from his cheek before he can stop her. The card treats the “old crush” as past enough to be talked about, but active enough to charge the present.

Contribution:

> **This card confirms that Makino was part of Haruko’s青春, not only her management history. Her affection for him began as an adolescent crush/admiration and matured into long-term trust, domestic intimacy, and recurring romantic play.**

## 5\. デートってこと！？ — romance work, one-sided love, and wanting to be someone’s first

The wedding/drama card builds directly on the prior romantic-comedy cards but becomes more emotionally direct.

Haruko has topped the “idol one would want as a bride” ranking, which is both flattering and uncomfortable because people around her are actually marrying. This gives her an adult-life comparison wound: fans idealize her as a bride, while her real romantic life remains mostly hypothetical. Then she receives a romance drama role involving mature恋愛経験, one-sided love, and a wedding dress — exactly the things she feels least qualified to embody. card\_hrk\_017\_st-card-hrk-05-wed…

At first she tries to prove she can do it without Makino. That matters. Haruko knows she has leaned on him for恋人ごっこ repeatedly, and she wants to show she has grown. But when the shoot goes badly — or she thinks it does — Makino suggests a cafe “date” practice, and she is delighted partly because this time he initiates it. Their cafe date echoes prior patterns: he chooses a drink he thinks she will like, she blushes because he considered her tastes, and then they slide into roleplay with cake-sharing, あーん, shopping, clothes, and pseudo-couple behavior.

The final drama scene is thematically pointed. Haruko’s character confesses to someone who loves another person, saying she knew she could never be his first, but that the love itself was precious. This clearly resonates beyond the drama. It evokes Haruko’s long history of watching Makino belong first to Mana, then to his role as manager for everyone. Haruko may not map one-to-one onto the drama role, but the writing uses the role to let her speak feelings adjacent to her own: love that does not demand possession, affection that is meaningful even if it does not become “number one.”

The poster-shoot substitute role makes Makino literally stand beside her in a wedding visual. Then the private ending is the real emotional payload: Haruko stumbles, rests against his back, thinks she wants to monopolize that back for just a little longer, and later says that someday she would like to be the number one of someone she likes.

That line is not subtle. It does not canonically name Makino as “the person,” but placed after a card full of Makino date practice, Makino as substitute groom figure, and Haruko leaning against his back, it is very hard not to read as at least partially directed through him.

Contribution:

> **This card articulates Haruko’s romantic wound: she is admired as an ideal bride by fans, but what she actually wants is not mass idealization — it is to be the first person in the heart of someone she loves.**

## 6\. 複雑な気持ち — work, ordinary youth, and Makino as the companion who makes choosing work less lonely

This Christmas card is excellent because it complicates Haruko’s idol dedication. She wants ordinary university life too. Her friends are planning parties and dates, and she is not immune to that. Makino notices her loneliness and initially tries to protect her rest by turning down a solo Christmas event. card\_hrk\_018\_st-card-hrk-05-xma…

But Haruko then sees two things: struggling young idols handing out flyers, and TRINITYAiLE practicing seriously for their own Christmas event. This forces her to remember her old self. When she was unknown, even one small job felt precious enough to tell her grandmother about. Seeing others fight for chances makes leisure feel wrong — not because leisure is morally bad, but because Haruko knows she still wants the work.

This is not simple workaholism. The card is careful: Haruko enjoys her friends and wanted to join them. But when faced with the actual choice, she realizes that idol work is where she feels most alive. Her friends understand and send her back. That matters because the story does not present ordinary friendship as inferior; it presents Haruko’s choice as clarified by people who love her.

The post-event wine scene is deeply Haruko/Makino. Makino buys the expensive wine she had wanted, framing it as a reward for the year. Haruko says university life is fun — trips, reports, cafeteria time, friends — but that she has realized she is happiest when working hard as an idol. Then she adds the key line: no matter how hard she works, Makino is beside her, and that makes her feel safe and happy.

She immediately realizes this is embarrassing, drinks, gets clingy, and says that if Makino is there, he will keep drinking with her, listening to her worries and complaints. This is one of the clearest versions of Haruko’s emotional dependency on him. She does not merely want him as a romantic object; she wants him as the person who makes choosing the idol path feel less lonely.

Contribution:

> **This card shows Haruko choosing idol life over ordinary Christmas not because she lacks ordinary desires, but because Makino’s companionship helps make the extraordinary life livable.**

## Batch synthesis

This batch is probably the strongest evidence so far that Haruko’s relationship with Makino is not just “trusted manager” or “old friend.” It is a long emotional history that began with adolescent awareness, passed through gratitude and survival support, became domestic trust, and now repeatedly expresses itself through date practice, food, wine, sleeping on him, flirtation, and her desire to be someone’s first.

The careful formulation is:

> **Haruko is not written as making an explicit current confession to Makino, but the text repeatedly frames Makino as the template through which she understands romance, safety, being cared for, and adult companionship.**

Her romantic coding is different from Rei’s. Rei’s romance coding is flustered, sharp, and self-concealing. Haruko’s is warmer, more adult, more playful, and more historically grounded. She knows the shape of what she is doing, but often underestimates how deeply it will affect her.

The big character updates:

**Haruko’s age insecurity becomes more precise.**
She is not simply embarrassed about being older. She was told she lacked freshness even when young, and now uniform costumes,恋愛仕事, and younger group members reopen that wound.

**Haruko’s romance inexperience is not immaturity.**
She missed ordinary dating because idol ambition consumed the years when other girls were experimenting with love. That makes her simultaneously adult and inexperienced.

**Makino is part of Haruko’s青春.**
The Valentine card explicitly says she once consciously noticed him. His praise, support, and happy reception of her chocolate became formative memories.

**Haruko’s ideal love is safety plus domesticity.**
She wants someone she can relax with, lean on, cook with, drink with, sleep near, and talk to without performing the perfect older-sister self.

**Haruko’s BIG4 arc continues.**
The pair card shows her accepting that she has become large enough to change other people’s behavior. She must learn to carry status without becoming either arrogant or embarrassed.

**Haruko’s idol choice is not costless.**
The Christmas card shows she wants normal adult friendships and seasonal youth, but ultimately chooses idol work because that is where she feels most herself — especially because Makino remains beside her.

## Priority ranking

**Core:**
ちょっと意識していた人; デートってこと！？; 複雑な気持ち.

**Very important:**
私をデートに連れてって; 一緒に頑張ろうって言ったでしょ？; むちっとはしてるけど.

All six are useful. None of these are throwaway, because even the comic/date-oriented ones define Haruko’s emotional grammar.

## Updated Haruko thesis after this batch

> **Saeki Haruko is not only Sunny Peace’s delayed-blooming older sister; she is the idol who learned to turn deferred ordinary life into performance. She missed normal youth, ordinary dating, easy confidence, and early recognition because she kept chasing idolhood from below Mana’s shadow. As a result, her adult self contains contradictions: she is dependable but lonely, maternal but romantically inexperienced, successful but still startled by status, playful but easily wounded by age and body comparisons. Makino is central because he witnessed the whole path — the failures, the crying, the small jobs, the chocolate, the cooking, the exhaustion, the dates-that-are-not-dates, and the choice to keep being an idol. Haruko’s deepest wish is not simply to be admired by fans as an ideal bride or older sister. It is to be seen, chosen, and allowed to lean without losing the strength that made her shine.**
