**Assistant — Turn 72**

I expanded Haruko’s archive and reviewed the full set as a visual corpus. Haruko’s visual language is very different from Sakura and Rei. Sakura is **life overflowing outward**; Rei is **discipline becoming grace**. Haruko is closer to **warmth that has waited too long to bloom**.

Her visual thesis is:

> **Saeki Haruko is designed as SUNNY PEACE’s delayed-blooming warmth: a soft, mature, emotionally generous idol whose visual language combines older-sister gentleness, domestic comfort, late-adolescent yearning, and a surprisingly rich desire to be seen as cute, feminine, glamorous, and personally cherished.**

## Core visual impression

Haruko’s core impression is **soft maturity**. She looks approachable and kind before anything else, but not in Sakura’s explosive, genki way. Haruko’s warmth is slower, gentler, and more enveloping. She often appears as someone who is already thinking about other people: feeding them, supporting them, celebrating them, guiding them, or quietly making the space more comfortable.

Her cards repeatedly emphasize:

-   warm indoor light;

-   cafés, parties, food, birthdays, festivals, and shared tables;

-   flowers and greenery;

-   soft smiles;

-   tilted, slightly bashful expressions;

-   gentle eye contact;

-   domestic or romantic atmospheres;

-   outfits that balance “cute idol” with “adult woman.”


The result is that Haruko feels like the emotional hearth of SUNNY PEACE. She is not the sun itself like Sakura, and not the refined dancer-line like Rei. She is the warmth you come home to.

## Hair / eyes / silhouette

Haruko has dark brown hair, usually worn around shoulder length with soft curls or gentle waves. Her hair is less graphically “iconic” than Sakura’s side-tail brightness or Rei’s long blonde dancer silhouette, but that is part of the point. It feels natural, touchable, and lived-in. Hairbands, ribbons, floral ornaments, and soft accessories often make her look carefully put together without feeling severe.

Her eyes are warm amber/gold-brown. They are gentle, slightly drooping, and often drawn with a soft gaze. Haruko’s eyes rarely confront the viewer aggressively. They invite, reassure, or quietly confess. Even in glamorous cards, her gaze usually carries tenderness rather than sharp seduction or icy confidence.

Her silhouette is rounded and feminine rather than angular. She is not visually built around speed like Sakura or line like Rei. Her body language tends to emphasize softness, curves, seated poses, leaning, reaching, and gentle physical presence. She often looks comfortable in domestic, romantic, or social spaces rather than only stage spaces.

This silhouette supports her “older-sister” role, but it also complicates it. Haruko does not simply look maternal. She also looks like someone who wants to be seen as a girl, as an idol, and as a romantic subject. That tension is one of the most important things in her visual design.

## Costume and color language

Haruko’s palette is built around **warm earth tones, soft greens, creams, yellows, floral whites, muted blues, and romantic pink/purple lighting**. Compared to Sakura’s saturated sun/festival palette and Rei’s blue-white dancer palette, Haruko’s colors often feel more grounded, seasonal, and intimate.

### Greens, creams, and florals

Haruko looks extremely natural in green, cream, and floral settings. The garden/birthday images, nature-like cards, and soft café interiors make her feel like a late-blooming flower. Green is important for her because it communicates growth, care, and delayed blooming rather than immediate radiance.

Her flower imagery is softer than Sakura’s. Sakura’s flowers often feel like celebration and life bursting forward. Haruko’s flowers feel like cultivation: something tended, waited for, and finally allowed to open.

### Yellow, orange, and warm indoor light

Haruko’s birthday, party, and café cards frequently use warm amber lighting. This is probably her most important visual environment. She thrives in spaces that feel inhabited: rooms with lamps, cakes, drinks, balloons, gifts, tables, or friends nearby. This gives her a domestic-social idol image rather than a purely stage-centered one.

The yellow/orange tones also tie her to SUNNY PEACE’s warmth, but in a more mature register. Sakura is sunlight; Haruko is lamplight.

### Blue and sailor colors

Her sailor and idol cards place her firmly inside SUNNY PEACE’s group language: bright, aquatic, cheerful, and energetic. But even there, Haruko tends to soften the composition. Her smile is inviting rather than explosive. She gives the group’s brightness emotional steadiness.

### Dark, mature, and romantic outfits

The adult/glamour, pajama, one-piece, Christmas, Valentine, and wedding-like cards show a different Haruko: not just caretaker, but a woman/girl who wants to be viewed as attractive, special, and chosen. These cards are important because they visually express a desire the text repeatedly gives her: she does not want to be reduced to “the reliable older sister.” She wants youth, beauty, romance, and specialness too.

The dark dress/card with red and black styling is especially significant. It shows that Haruko can carry a more dramatic, mature visual mode. It does not feel like her default self, but it reveals an aspiration: “I can be glamorous too.”

## Body language / facial expression tendencies

Haruko’s body language is softer and more relational than Rei’s. She is often:

-   leaning toward someone;

-   offering food or gifts;

-   sitting in a shared space;

-   smiling with closed or softened eyes;

-   reaching gently rather than dramatically;

-   turned partially toward another character;

-   framed in scenes of hospitality, celebration, or after-work intimacy.


She has fewer “heroic forward thrust” poses than Sakura and fewer “perfect body-line” poses than Rei. Haruko’s body language says, **I am here with you**, not **look at me alone**.

Her expressions have a wide but gentle range:

### Warm smile

This is her baseline. Haruko’s smile often looks practiced but sincere: the smile of someone used to reassuring others.

### Bashful softness

Many cards show her blushing, looking aside, or smiling with slight embarrassment. This supports her delayed-blooming theme. She wants to be cute or desired, but she is not always confident claiming that.

### Mature calm

In some cards, especially dressier or more formal ones, Haruko carries a calm adult poise. She looks like the member who can host, guide, or stabilize a room.

### Wistful vulnerability

The one-piece/bathing, pajama, Christmas, and wedding-like cards carry a quieter mood. She often looks more introspective there — not unhappy, but aware of longing. These images align with the textual Haruko who thinks about missed youth, ordinary adulthood, romance, and the desire to be someone’s first choice.

## Public idol image

Haruko’s public idol image is **the healing older-sister idol** — but that phrase needs care, because “older sister” can flatten her.

Publicly, she gives SUNNY PEACE:

-   emotional steadiness;

-   social warmth;

-   approachable maturity;

-   a sense of care;

-   gentle beauty;

-   reliability;

-   hospitality;

-   softness that makes the group feel safe.


She is the member who makes SUNNY PEACE feel like a place rather than only a performance unit. Sakura makes it shine; Rei gives it form; Chisa gives it earnest vulnerability; Shizuku gives it fan-memory and receptive attention; Haruko makes it feel inhabitable.

Visually, she is often surrounded by food, gifts, party décor, flowers, cafés, and warm interiors. That makes her public image close to “the idol who welcomes you.” She is not a distant star. She is the person who makes the table ready before everyone arrives.

## Private / casual design cues

Haruko’s private/casual cards are among the most important in her archive because they reveal the gap between her role and her wants.

In casual images, she often appears in:

-   schoolwear;

-   soft sweaters;

-   aprons or food-associated scenes;

-   relaxed feminine clothing;

-   pajama/loungewear;

-   café or party settings;

-   shopping/festival scenes;

-   romantic or seasonal outfits.


These designs strongly reinforce her domestic and social intelligence. She looks natural cooking, eating, preparing, celebrating, talking, and supporting. But the same archive also keeps placing her in visually intimate scenarios where she is not only the caretaker: she is the one being seen, dressed up, admired, or gently vulnerable.

That is crucial. Haruko’s visual language repeatedly says:

> She is good at caring for others, but she also wants to be cared for.
> She is good at making others shine, but she also wants to bloom.
> She is “older-sister-like,” but she is not finished growing.

## Design archetypes evoked

Haruko evokes several overlapping archetypes.

### The nurturing older sister

This is the most obvious: warm eyes, soft smile, domestic contexts, food, reassurance, and reliable posture. She reads as someone who supports the younger/more anxious members.

### The delayed-blooming idol

This is the deeper archetype. Haruko often appears in flower, garden, birthday, and seasonal imagery that suggests gradual blooming rather than instant genius. Her beauty feels cultivated through time.

### The domestic hearth idol

Food, cafés, parties, gifts, warm interiors, and soft clothing make her feel like an idol of shared everyday life. She does not only shine on stage; she shines while making ordinary spaces emotionally meaningful.

### The romantic heroine who arrived late

The wedding-like, Valentine, Christmas, one-piece, and pajama imagery all give Haruko a gentle romantic charge. She is not drawn as a purely innocent girl or a purely maternal figure. She is someone who wants to be chosen, admired, and desired in a soft, emotionally meaningful way.

### The “mature but cute” idol

Haruko’s archive repeatedly toggles between mature beauty and cute idol display. This is very important. Her design refuses to let “mature” mean “past her time.” She can be adult-coded, cute, bashful, glamorous, and earnest all at once.

## How the design supports personality and story role

Haruko’s story role is that of the **delayed flower**: the girl who lacked obvious early sparkle, watched brighter idols from below, feared being ordinary or too late, and gradually learns that her warmth, steadiness, care, and persistence are not consolation prizes but her actual idol material.

Her visual design supports that through repeated contrasts.

### Caregiver vs. girl who wants care

Many images show her in caring or hosting spaces. But others place her in vulnerable, romantic, or self-conscious compositions. This reinforces her core contradiction: she can play the mature role, but she does not want to be trapped in it.

### Warmth vs. glamour

Haruko’s natural register is warmth. But the adult, Christmas, Valentine, pajama, and wedding-like cards test glamour. The archive asks: can Haruko also be dramatic, alluring, special, and personally beautiful? The answer is yes — but her glamour remains soft rather than cold.

### Ordinary life vs. idol transformation

Haruko’s casual scenes make her feel ordinary in the best sense: someone who belongs in kitchens, cafés, classrooms, festivals, and shared rooms. Her idol-stage cards transform that ordinariness into performance. This supports her thesis: Haruko’s idol power comes from everyday warmth elevated into stage radiance.

### Late blooming vs. being “too late”

The flower and garden cards are especially important because they visually reject the idea that Haruko missed her chance. She is not a faded flower. She is a flower that needed time and care to open.

## Haruko compared with Sakura and Rei

The three SUNNY PEACE visual profiles are now very clear.

Sakura says:

> **I am alive, and I want to share this joy with you.**

Rei says:

> **I have trained myself into form, and now I am learning to let that form carry joy.**

Haruko says:

> **I have been waiting to bloom, and I want the warmth I give others to become my own light too.**

Sakura is sun. Rei is graceful motion. Haruko is hearth and flower. Sakura pulls you into joy; Rei makes joy elegant; Haruko makes joy feel safe enough to inhabit.

## Final visual thesis

Haruko Saeki’s card illustrations present her as **SUNNY PEACE’s delayed-blooming warmth**: a soft-eyed, dark-haired, gently feminine idol whose design blends domestic comfort, mature beauty, flower imagery, social care, and quiet longing.

Her warm palettes, café and party settings, food imagery, floral costumes, soft smiles, rounded silhouette, and intimate seasonal outfits all reinforce her role as the group’s emotional stabilizer. But the archive also insists that Haruko is not merely a caretaker. She wants to be cute, glamorous, romantic, admired, and chosen. Her visual power lies in that tension.

In short:

> **Haruko’s design says that kindness can be radiance, ordinary warmth can become idol light, and a girl who thought she bloomed too late can still become someone’s most beautiful flower.**

**Assistant — Turn 74**

I expanded Chisa’s archive and reviewed the full illustration set. Chisa’s visual language is very coherent: she is designed around **smallness, softness, careful effort, fear transformed into charm, and the gradual discovery that a timid girl can still occupy the stage**.

Her visual thesis is:

> **Shiraishi Chisa is designed as SUNNY PEACE’s handmade courage: a small, soft, anxious-looking idol whose visual identity turns timidity, craft, horror-cuteness, sisterly admiration, and earnest effort into a form of approachable radiance.**

## Core visual impression

Chisa’s core visual impression is **fragile-but-trying**. She is visually much less forceful than Sakura, less physically elegant than Rei, and less maturely grounded than Haruko. Her appeal is not built around overwhelming presence. Instead, she often looks like someone who is still gathering courage: looking up, clutching something, leaning slightly inward, sitting low in the frame, or appearing surprised that the viewer is seeing her.

But the archive does not present her as weak in a purely helpless way. The more cards you look at, the more her softness becomes active. Chisa’s design repeatedly says:

> “I may be scared, but I am here. I am trying. Please keep watching.”

That matches her story role exactly. Chisa’s idolhood is built from visible effort, not effortless charisma.

## Hair / eyes / silhouette

Chisa’s hair is one of her strongest identifiers: short, soft, light blonde to ash-blonde, with long bangs that often cover or partially obscure one eye. This asymmetry is extremely important. It gives her a shy, hidden, slightly withdrawn impression. One eye is visible; one is partly concealed. Visually, she is always half-emerging from behind herself.

That design choice supports her whole character. Chisa is someone with feelings, tastes, effort, and courage inside her, but she often struggles to show them directly. Her hair makes concealment cute, but it also makes it psychologically meaningful.

Her visible eye is large, purple, and very expressive. Compared to Sakura’s open amber warmth or Rei’s composed green gaze, Chisa’s eye tends to look more vulnerable: uncertain, sparkling, anxious, surprised, or carefully hopeful. Her gaze often feels like it is asking for reassurance.

Her silhouette is compact and rounded. She is visually small, with soft shoulders, short hair, frilled costumes, oversized accessories, ribbons, plushes, and costumes that often make her look doll-like or handmade. Even when she wears more dramatic outfits, the silhouette rarely becomes severe. It remains petite, decorated, and slightly fragile.

This supports her “empathy idol” role. Chisa looks like someone fans can want to protect — but her arc is that she gradually becomes someone who can protect and encourage others too.

## Costume and color language

Chisa’s palette is built around **lavender, pink, pale yellow, mint, white, soft blue, and pastel green**, with occasional black, red, and neon accents when the card wants to pull her into horror, rock, or more dramatic imagery.

### Pastels and softness

Her default colors are gentle: pinks, lavenders, creams, pale blues, and soft greens. These colors make her feel sweet, harmless, approachable, and emotionally delicate. They also connect her to handmade craft imagery: ribbons, lace, plushes, frills, candy-like accessories, and small decorative details.

Where Sakura’s palette says “sun and festival,” Chisa’s says “handmade gift, plush room, soft towel, small flower, careful decoration.”

### Horror-cute / gothic colors

The Alice card and ghostly birthday card are very important because they show Chisa’s horror interest visually. The Alice card uses black, white, red, deep purple, roses, rabbits, doll-like staging, and eerie sweets. It does not make Chisa monstrous; it makes her look like a timid girl who has wandered into a horror-fairy-tale set and somehow belongs there.

This is perfect for her. Chisa’s horror love is not edgy coolness. It is fear converted into play, aesthetic, and craft. The visual language becomes “scary but cute,” which is exactly how Chisa metabolizes fear.

### Aquatic and airy blues

Her aquarium/underwater card is one of her prettiest visual modes. The blue dress, fish, coral, bubbles, pearls, and flowing composition make Chisa look like a small mermaid or aquarium princess. This emphasizes her gentleness and vulnerability, but also gives her an almost dreamlike stage presence.

### Yellow-green / domestic craft colors

The maid and café-like cards use warmer yellows, greens, whites, and browns. These make her feel handmade, domestic, and slightly old-fashioned. They fit her sewing/costume/craft side very well. She looks like someone who belongs in a room full of little objects, decorations, sweets, tools, and lovingly arranged details.

### Rock / black-pink mode

The rock card is a major contrast. Chisa wears black, mesh-like textures, pink accents, bold hair clips, and grips the microphone with much more direct force. This card is important because it shows that Chisa can visually access a more assertive self. But even here, she does not become a hard-edged rock star in the IIIX/kana sense. She still looks like Chisa discovering power inside herself, not like someone who has always owned the room.

## Body language / facial expression tendencies

Chisa’s body language tends to be inward, careful, or object-mediated.

She often:

-   clutches an object close;

-   holds food, fabric, plushes, tools, a microphone, or props;

-   sits or curls into the composition;

-   looks upward toward the viewer;

-   covers or partially hides her face;

-   has one hand near her mouth;

-   leans into friends or lets friends lean into her;

-   appears caught mid-reaction rather than fully commanding the scene.


This is very different from Sakura’s outward reach and Rei’s dancer-line control. Chisa’s posture says she is still negotiating with the fact of being seen.

Her expressions tend toward:

-   shy smiles;

-   startled softness;

-   anxious concentration;

-   gentle joy;

-   tearful or overwhelmed delight;

-   closed-eye happiness when supported by others;

-   occasional intense focus when performing.


The key is that Chisa rarely looks emotionally opaque. Even when she is shy, the viewer understands what she is feeling. Her design makes vulnerability legible.

## Public idol image

Chisa’s public idol image is **the timid-but-earnest support idol**.

She is not the center-sun like Sakura, the graceful dancer like Rei, the warm older-sister figure like Haruko, or the fan-translator like Shizuku. Chisa’s visual appeal is that she looks like someone growing right in front of the audience.

Her idol image depends on:

-   sincerity;

-   visible effort;

-   small courage;

-   cuteness that does not feel aggressive;

-   emotional accessibility;

-   handmade charm;

-   “I’m scared, but I’ll try” energy.


This is why her cheerleader card is especially revealing. Cheerleader imagery normally implies outward confidence, loud support, and performative brightness. But Chisa’s version still contains softness and slight uncertainty. She is cheering, but she is also being brave by cheering.

Her birthday and stage cards push her forward visually — reaching, singing, surrounded by confetti or lights — but they still preserve her underlying vulnerability. That is the point. Chisa does not become compelling by erasing the timid girl. She becomes compelling by letting the timid girl stand onstage.

## Private / casual design cues

Chisa’s casual and private cards emphasize three things: **friendship, craft, and comfort objects**.

She appears with food, school towels, pajamas, plushes, yukata, shopping bags, casual sweaters, and group meals. These scenes make her feel like someone whose idol identity is deeply connected to ordinary reassurance: eating with others, being included, wearing matching clothes, making or choosing outfits, having a cozy room, going to events with friends.

The pajama/plush card is especially important. Chisa is surrounded by stuffed animals, pastel bedding, soft textures, and toy-like objects. It makes her look childlike, but not in a shallow way. It communicates that she needs softness around her. Her private world is a buffer against fear.

The yukata card gives her a more festive, gently confident look. It is still cute and soft, but there is a clearer forward-facing smile. It reads as Chisa participating in seasonal life rather than hiding from it.

The school/casual friend images are also crucial because Chisa often appears socially embedded. She is rarely isolated in a cold, dramatic vacuum. She is with Saki, Sakura, Shizuku, or other girls. This supports her story: Chisa grows through standing beside others.

## Design archetypes evoked

Chisa evokes several overlapping archetypes.

### The shy little-sister idol

This is the most obvious. Her size, short hair, partial face concealment, soft colors, and anxious expressions make her read as the younger-sister figure who needs encouragement.

### The handmade craft girl

Ribbons, frills, sewing-like details, costume-conscious cards, lace, plushes, and carefully arranged props all support her craft identity. She feels like someone who understands love through making things.

### The horror-cute girl

The Alice, ghost, gothic, and slightly eerie cards reveal a delightful contradiction: Chisa is scared easily, but she is also fascinated by fear. Her design handles horror through cuteness rather than darkness alone.

### The anxious performer becoming brave

The idol and rock cards show the core arc. Chisa is not born commanding the stage; she has to step into it. Her performance images are therefore most powerful when they still show traces of nervousness.

### The soft mascot/plush-room girl

Her pastel, pajama, stuffed-animal, and round accessory imagery makes her extremely mascot-compatible. Unlike Sakura, whose mascot affinity is cheerful and outward, Chisa’s is comfort-based. Plushes are safe companions.

### The small flower

Her floral cards, especially the hydrangea/blue flower imagery, position her as a small bloom rather than a sunburst. She is delicate, but carefully arranged and beautiful when given the right environment.

## How the design supports personality and story role

Chisa’s design supports her character almost perfectly because it visualizes her central contradiction:

> She looks like someone who needs support, but her story is about becoming support.

Her covered eye, small frame, pastel palette, inward posture, and soft expressions all establish her vulnerability. But her stage cards, cheer cards, rock card, and duet/partner imagery show that vulnerability becoming communicable strength.

This connects directly to her text characterization. Chisa is the member whose fear becomes empathy. Because she knows what it is like to be scared, inadequate, slow, and dependent, she can stand beside fans and say: “Let’s keep trying together.”

The visual design reinforces this by never making her transformation look like a total replacement. Chisa does not become Sakura. She does not become Rei. She does not become a coldly confident professional. She remains visibly Chisa: small, soft, shy, careful, and decorated with the language of handmade affection.

That is why her stronger cards work. The rock card is not “Chisa stops being Chisa.” It is “Chisa finds a sharper Chisa inside herself.” The blue flower card is not “Chisa becomes adult glamour.” It is “Chisa’s delicacy is staged as beauty.” The maid and pajama cards are not just cute fanservice; they reinforce her domestic/craft/comfort identity.

## Chisa compared with Sakura, Rei, and Haruko

The SUNNY PEACE visual map is now very clear.

Sakura says:

> **I am alive, and I want to share this joy with you.**

Rei says:

> **I have trained myself into form, and now I am learning to let that form carry joy.**

Haruko says:

> **I have been waiting to bloom, and I want the warmth I give others to become my own light too.**

Chisa says:

> **I am scared, but I will keep trying — and maybe watching me try can help you keep trying too.**

Sakura is sunlight. Rei is disciplined grace. Haruko is hearth and flower. Chisa is small courage made visible.

## Final visual thesis

Chisa Shiraishi’s card illustrations present her as **SUNNY PEACE’s handmade courage**: a short-haired, purple-eyed, soft-bodied, pastel-coded idol whose visual identity is built from shyness, craft, fear, plush comfort, sisterly warmth, and earnest effort.

Her covered eye, compact silhouette, gentle colors, frilled costumes, horror-cute motifs, plushes, flowers, and careful body language all reinforce the idea that she begins from timidity rather than command. But the archive repeatedly lets that timidity become stage power: she cheers, sings, reaches, dresses up, performs, and even accesses sharper rock and elegant flower modes without losing her fundamental softness.

In short:

> **Chisa’s design says that idol radiance does not have to begin as confidence. It can begin as fear, effort, a handmade costume, a soft plush, a trembling voice, and the decision to stand where people can see you anyway.**

I generated a review sheet while inspecting the archive: Chisa card contact sheet.

**Assistant — Turn 76**

I expanded Shizuku’s archive and reviewed the full illustration set as a visual corpus. Shizuku’s design is extremely revealing because she does not visually function like the other SUNNY PEACE members. Sakura is **life overflowing outward**; Rei is **discipline becoming grace**; Haruko is **warmth that waited to bloom**; Chisa is **small courage made visible**. Shizuku is something quieter:

> **Hyōdō Shizuku is designed as the fan who became an idol: a soft, pastel, receptive girl whose visual identity turns admiration, memory, observation, and gentle emotional absorption into idol radiance.**

## Core visual impression

Shizuku’s core impression is **soft receptivity**. She is not usually drawn as the loudest person in the scene, nor as the most physically commanding. Even when she is in highly colorful, playful, or theatrical cards, she tends to feel like someone who is **receiving the world intensely** before responding to it.

Her illustrations repeatedly emphasize:

-   pastel color fields;

-   plushes, magazines, mascot goods, penlights, cards, and fan objects;

-   soft blue hair and pink-purple eyes;

-   indoor/private hobby spaces;

-   dreamy stage lighting;

-   slightly parted lips or wide-eyed surprise;

-   gentle reaching gestures rather than explosive poses;

-   scenes where she is beside, near, or emotionally oriented toward another person.


This makes Shizuku feel like SUNNY PEACE’s **emotional antenna**. She notices, absorbs, treasures, and reflects. Her radiance does not begin as command; it begins as appreciation.

## Hair / eyes / silhouette

Shizuku’s hair is pale aqua-blue, usually arranged in twin buns, side tails, ribbons, or soft curled sections. It gives her a distinctive fluffy, rounded, almost cloudlike silhouette. Compared with Sakura’s warm honey-brown brightness, Rei’s long blonde dancer-line, Haruko’s dark domestic softness, and Chisa’s short hidden-eye vulnerability, Shizuku’s hair makes her feel airy, dreamy, and slightly doll-like.

Her eyes are pinkish-red to purple-red, large and very soft. They are not icy or severe. They often make her look as though she is quietly overwhelmed by what she is seeing: a stage, a friend, a fan moment, a beautiful place, or a cherished object. This is crucial to her design. Shizuku’s gaze often looks **moved** before it looks assertive.

Her silhouette is petite, rounded, and decorative. Twin buns, hair ornaments, ribbons, puffed sleeves, frills, mascot-like accessories, and pastel costumes all make her visually compact and cute. But unlike Chisa’s smallness, which communicates fear and effort, Shizuku’s smallness communicates **cherishing**. She looks like someone who keeps emotional things close.

## Costume and color language

Shizuku’s palette is built around **aqua, mint, lavender, pink, white, pale yellow, and candy-like saturated accents**. Her colors are among the most consistently “goods-like” in SUNNY PEACE: they feel like idol merch, pastel stationery, acrylic stands, plush packaging, magazine layouts, and fan-event decorations.

### Aqua / mint / blue

This is her base language. Aqua gives her a cool, soft, clean impression, but not a severe one. It makes her feel calm, waterlike, and emotionally reflective. In the pool, sailor, angel, and idol cards, blue and aqua make her feel like someone connected to air, water, light, and gentle motion.

### Pink / lavender / pastel toy colors

Many of her birthday and mascot-heavy cards use pink, lavender, candy colors, ribbons, plushes, stars, balloons, and toy-like props. This gives Shizuku a strong “fan goods” atmosphere. She looks extremely compatible with merch, mascot collabs, idol magazines, and collectible imagery.

That matters because Shizuku is textually connected to fandom. Her visual design does not merely make her “cute”; it makes her look like someone who understands the emotional power of cute objects, goods, magazines, and saved memories.

### White / angelic colors

The angel card and some stage costumes lean into white, pale blue, and light effects. These give her a dreamy, almost heavenly softness. But unlike Mana’s “miracle” aura, Shizuku’s angelic quality is not mythic or tragic. It is more like a fan’s idealized dream of idol cuteness made gentle and approachable.

### Dark / rock colors

The rock card is a major contrast. Black, neon green, purple, and stage-sign lighting pull Shizuku into a sharper public image. But even there, she remains seated low, holding a megaphone, framed as a small figure inside a loud environment. The card does not erase her softness; it shows that her quietness can be amplified.

## Body language / facial expression tendencies

Shizuku’s body language is usually less aggressive than Sakura’s and less technically composed than Rei’s. She often:

-   sits low or curls inward;

-   leans close to another person;

-   looks upward or sideways;

-   holds objects near her body;

-   reaches gently rather than dramatically;

-   appears caught in a moment of wonder;

-   smiles softly or with delighted surprise;

-   performs with sincerity rather than dominance.


Her facial expressions are especially important. She frequently has an open, slightly astonished expression, as if the world is giving her something precious. In other cards, she smiles with a gentle, almost private happiness. She is visually at her most powerful when the image suggests that she is not simply “being cute,” but **feeling something deeply and letting the viewer see it**.

This supports her role as SUNNY PEACE’s fan-to-idol bridge. Shizuku looks like someone who remembers what it means to be in the audience.

## Public idol image

Shizuku’s public idol image is **the gentle dream-fan idol**.

She gives SUNNY PEACE a very specific kind of softness. Sakura makes the unit shine outward; Rei gives the unit form; Haruko makes the unit warm and inhabitable; Chisa makes fear and effort lovable. Shizuku gives SUNNY PEACE the feeling that the stage is something to be **treasured**.

Her public image depends on:

-   emotional receptivity;

-   pastel cuteness;

-   dreamy softness;

-   fan empathy;

-   quiet sincerity;

-   mascot/goods compatibility;

-   the sense that she loves idol culture from the inside.


This makes her different from a generic “quiet girl” archetype. Shizuku is not merely passive. She is a girl whose power comes from **valuing**. She notices what is worth preserving. She understands how an idol moment becomes a memory in someone else’s life.

Her magazine/fan-layout imagery is especially central. It visually places her between the idol and the fan archive: photos, pages, notes, layouts, saved images, and the act of looking. Shizuku is not only someone who appears in idol media; she is someone who understands why a fan would keep idol media close.

## Private / casual design cues

Shizuku’s private/casual design cues are among the most revealing in the SUNNY PEACE set.

The school/casual scene with Rei shows her physically close, tucked into shared space, looking at a device or object together. It reinforces her intimacy style: quiet, near, companionable, not loud.

The penlight/concert card is perhaps one of the most important visually. Shizuku with glowsticks is Shizuku in her original grammar: fan energy. She does not look like an idol standing above fandom; she looks like someone who has inhabited fandom and still carries that joy into her idol self.

The pajama/card-game image, with glasses and soft room lighting, gives her a private “hobby girl” feeling. This is not the same as Chisa’s plush-safe-room softness. Shizuku’s private world feels like **collection, play, memory, and quiet enthusiasm**.

The pool, beach, yukata, and outdoor cards show that she can participate in seasonal idol imagery, but she usually retains a slightly reserved or contemplative air. She is present, but rarely loses the impression of inward processing.

## Design archetypes evoked

Shizuku evokes several overlapping archetypes.

### The fan who became an idol

This is her central visual archetype. Magazine layouts, glowsticks, plushes, collectible-like colors, and goods-heavy compositions repeatedly link her to the audience side of idol culture.

### The soft mascot-compatible idol

Shizuku works naturally with plushes, rabbits, bears, stars, pastel animals, and collab-style imagery. But unlike Sakura’s mascot energy, which is outgoing and festival-like, Shizuku’s is more collectible and cherished. She feels like someone who would lovingly arrange goods on a shelf.

### The dreamy pastel girl

Aqua hair, lavender-pink eyes, soft lighting, angel wings, ribbons, and floating compositions make her feel slightly dreamlike. She often looks like she belongs inside a fan’s imagined perfect idol world.

### The quiet emotional interpreter

Her gaze and posture suggest observation before action. She does not simply react; she receives and interprets. This supports her narrative function as someone who understands feelings, fandom, and memory.

### The hobby-room / otaku-adjacent girl

Glasses, cards, magazines, glowsticks, and private-room imagery give her a subtle otaku/fan identity without making her a parody. Her fandom feels sincere, not grotesque or comic.

### The small-stage miracle

Her idol close-up, with green penlights behind her, is one of the strongest images in the set. It frames her not as a grand mythic miracle, but as a small, emotionally overwhelmed girl becoming the focus of many lights. That is Shizuku’s idolhood in miniature: the fan who once looked up is now being looked up to.

## How the design supports personality and story role

Shizuku’s design supports her story role because it visualizes the movement from **observer to participant**.

She is not designed as someone who naturally dominates space. Her body language often remains gentle, low, near, or receptive. Her palette is soft and fan-goods-like. Her props are frequently objects of affection: magazines, plushes, penlights, sweets, cards, costumes, mascots. Her face often looks as if she is being emotionally reached by the world.

That is exactly what makes her important.

Shizuku’s idolhood is not about proving that she was secretly the strongest or loudest. It is about proving that someone who loves idols from the audience side can become an idol without betraying that love. She carries the fan’s gaze onto the stage.

Her visual design says:

> The person holding the penlight can become the person standing under the penlights.
> The person saving magazines can become the person printed in magazines.
> The person treasuring an idol can become someone treasured by fans.

That is why her softness is not incidental. It is the point. Shizuku’s radiance is reflective rather than solar. She shines because she has absorbed so much light from others and learned to return it.

## Shizuku compared with the rest of SUNNY PEACE

The SUNNY PEACE visual map is now very clear.

Sakura says:

> **I am alive, and I want to share this joy with you.**

Rei says:

> **I have trained myself into form, and now I am learning to let that form carry joy.**

Haruko says:

> **I have been waiting to bloom, and I want the warmth I give others to become my own light too.**

Chisa says:

> **I am scared, but I will keep trying — and maybe watching me try can help you keep trying too.**

Shizuku says:

> **I loved idols first, and now I want to return that love from the stage.**

That makes her indispensable to SUNNY PEACE. Without Sakura, the unit loses its sun. Without Rei, it loses form. Without Haruko, it loses hearth. Without Chisa, it loses small visible courage. Without Shizuku, it loses the memory of what idol light feels like from below.

## Final visual thesis

Shizuku Hyōdō’s card illustrations present her as **SUNNY PEACE’s fan-memory idol**: a pale-aqua-haired, pastel-coded, soft-eyed girl whose visual identity is built from admiration, receptivity, quiet enthusiasm, idol goods, mascots, magazines, glowsticks, and dreamy emotional closeness.

Her design repeatedly places her between two positions: the girl who watches and the girl who is watched. She is surrounded by the visual materials of fandom — pages, plushes, penlights, collectibles, seasonal memories — but the cards gradually show that she can stand inside those materials as an idol herself.

In short:

> **Shizuku’s design says that idolhood is not only the act of shining. It is also the act of remembering what it felt like to be reached by someone else’s light, then stepping onto the stage so that feeling can be returned.**

**Assistant — Turn 78**

Here is a standalone reference document consolidating the SUNNY PEACE member analyses, including personality, voice, characterization, and visual design.

# SUNNY PEACE: Member Personality, Voice, Characterization, and Visual Reference

## IDOLY PRIDE Comparative Analysis Document

## Purpose of this document

This document provides a detailed reference for the five members of **SUNNY PEACE** from *IDOLY PRIDE*: **Kawasaki Sakura**, **Ichinose Rei**, **Saeki Haruko**, **Shiraishi Chisa**, and **Hyōdō Shizuku**. It is intended to function as an authoritative source for future comparative analysis of character design, personality, performance identity, narrative role, voice, interpersonal dynamics, and visual storytelling.

The focus is not merely “what each girl is like.” The goal is to define how each member contributes a distinct form of light to SUNNY PEACE, and how the unit’s shared philosophy depends on those differences.

SUNNY PEACE can be summarized as:

> **A unit of girls who could not shine alone, but learn to shine by becoming one another’s proof.**

Their collective image is bright, cheerful, and accessible, but the group is not shallowly optimistic. SUNNY PEACE is built from illness, failure, loneliness, delayed youth, fear, admiration, and the need to be supported. Their smiles matter because they are not effortless. They are chosen, practiced, protected, and returned to fans as warmth.

At the broadest level:

-   **Sakura** is life overflowing outward.

-   **Rei** is discipline becoming grace.

-   **Haruko** is warmth that waited too long to bloom.

-   **Chisa** is small courage made visible.

-   **Shizuku** is fandom, memory, and admiration returned from the stage.


Together, they form a unit whose idolhood is not based on overwhelming individual talent or mythic destiny, but on **reciprocal emotional support**: each girl is able to stand because the others help her stand, and each girl’s weakness becomes part of the group’s communicable light.

---

# I. SUNNY PEACE as a unit

## 1\. Core unit identity

SUNNY PEACE is the “sun” unit of Hoshimi Production, but their sunlight should not be mistaken for simple cheerfulness. Their brightness is not the brightness of girls who have never suffered. It is the brightness of girls who have suffered, been delayed, frightened, overlooked, or made dependent — and who gradually learn to turn those experiences into warmth for others.

SUNNY PEACE’s core emotional grammar is:

> **I was supported, so now I want to support someone else.**

This is true for all five members.

Sakura was supported through illness, family love, Mana’s inherited miracle, and the people who helped her live after transplant. Her smile becomes gratitude made performative.

Rei was supported by the rediscovery of dance as joy rather than proof. SUNNY PEACE teaches her that disciplined movement can become shared happiness.

Haruko was supported by an idol dream that waited for her across time. Her warmth becomes a shelter for others because she knows what it means to think one has missed one’s chance.

Chisa was supported by her sister, by the group, by fans, and by small acts of encouragement. Her fear becomes empathy.

Shizuku was supported by idols from the audience side. Her fandom becomes a bridge between fan and performer.

The unit’s name is therefore almost literal. “SUNNY” is not only Sakura’s sun. “PEACE” is not only gentleness. The group’s peace is an emotionally survivable space — a place where girls who might otherwise have withdrawn, overtrained, hidden, delayed themselves, or remained observers can stand together and shine.

## 2\. SUNNY PEACE’s performance philosophy

SUNNY PEACE’s performance philosophy is based on **returning received feeling**. They do not simply project charisma downward from stage to audience. They receive encouragement, fan support, friendship, and memory, then return it as song, smile, and presence.

Their idolhood is therefore circular rather than vertical.

A purely vertical idol model says:

> The idol shines.
> The audience receives.

SUNNY PEACE’s model says:

> The audience supports the idol.
> The idol receives that feeling.
> The idol returns it as warmth.
> The audience is encouraged in turn.

This is why their image depends so heavily on openness, accessibility, food, festivals, casual intimacy, emotional vulnerability, flowers, pastel colors, and public smiles. SUNNY PEACE are not meant to feel unreachable. They are meant to feel like an emotional place the fan can enter.

## 3\. The five-member emotional structure

Each SUNNY PEACE member contributes a different kind of emotional light.

**Sakura** provides solar immediacy. She is the person whose joy pulls others forward.

**Rei** provides disciplined form. She turns the group’s warmth into polished performance.

**Haruko** provides mature warmth. She makes the group feel safe, social, and emotionally inhabited.

**Chisa** provides visible effort. She lets fans see fear becoming courage.

**Shizuku** provides fan-memory. She remembers what idol light feels like from below and brings that memory onto the stage.

The unit fails if any one of these functions is removed. Without Sakura, SUNNY PEACE loses its central sun. Without Rei, that sun lacks form. Without Haruko, the warmth lacks hearth. Without Chisa, the group loses its small, effortful courage. Without Shizuku, it loses its memory of fandom itself.

---

# II. Kawasaki Sakura

## 1\. Core thesis

**Kawasaki Sakura is SUNNY PEACE’s survivor-sun: a life-affirming idol whose brightness is inseparable from ordinary embodiment, gratitude, appetite, and the fact that she is still alive.**

Sakura is the emotional center of SUNNY PEACE and one of the central figures of *IDOLY PRIDE* as a whole. Her story cannot be reduced to “cheerful protagonist” or “Mana’s successor.” She is a girl who survived serious illness, received a heart transplant, inherited a connection to Nagase Mana, and had to fight the danger of being treated as Mana’s vessel rather than Kawasaki Sakura.

Her defining movement is:

> **from miracle-receiver to living person;
> from Mana’s possible replacement to Sakura herself;
> from girl saved by others to idol who wants to make others feel alive.**

Sakura’s happiness matters because it is embodied. She eats, laughs, reaches, visits local places, loves mascots, responds intensely to food, enjoys seasonal events, and throws herself into the world. Her joy is not abstract. It is the joy of someone who knows ordinary life is not guaranteed.

## 2\. Personality

Sakura is warm, energetic, emotionally direct, curious, affectionate, and intensely responsive. She is a classic 元気 / genki personality on the surface: lively, bright, enthusiastic, and easy to read. But her genki quality has depth because it grows from survival rather than innocence.

She is not cheerful because she lacks pain. She is cheerful because being able to experience joy feels precious to her.

Her personality is marked by several recurring traits.

First, she has **immediacy**. Sakura reacts quickly and openly. If she is excited, hungry, moved, embarrassed, or happy, everyone can tell. She is not a character who habitually conceals emotion behind analysis. She feels first, then thinks.

Second, she has **appetite**. This is not a trivial gag. Sakura’s love of food is one of her deepest proof-of-life motifs. Eating means having a body that can enjoy the world. Food scenes, local treats, restaurants, and festival meals all support her characterization as someone whose miracle is ordinary life restored.

Third, she has **social warmth**. Sakura naturally pulls people into shared experience. She wants others to enjoy things with her. She does not simply say, “I am happy.” Her instinct is closer to, “Isn’t this wonderful? Let’s enjoy it together.”

Fourth, she has **survivor urgency**. Sakura’s desire to perform can become dangerous because she treats stages as precious, possibly unrepeatable moments. This gives her idolhood extraordinary sincerity, but it also creates the risk that she will overextend herself. Her brightness has a cost.

## 3\. Voice and speech style

Sakura’s voice is open, bright, emotionally transparent, and highly reactive. She tends to speak with warmth and immediacy rather than calculation. Her speech often feels like it arrives directly from feeling.

She is likely to use enthusiastic reactions, direct praise, and emotionally simple but sincere statements. She is not unintelligent, but she is not verbally ornate in the way a more self-conscious performer might be. Her language is accessible. She says what she feels in a way that makes others feel invited rather than judged.

Her vocal identity can be summarized as:

> **warm immediacy.**

She speaks as someone who wants to close distance. Even when she is anxious or burdened, she tends to express emotion in a way that calls others toward her. Her idol voice is therefore a natural extension of her ordinary voice: bright, direct, life-affirming, and relational.

## 4\. Characterization and story role

Sakura’s central narrative function is to humanize miracle.

Nagase Mana represents miracle as myth: the legendary idol who died and left behind an afterimage so powerful that everyone must respond to it. Sakura is connected to that miracle through her heart and voice, but the story’s ethical task is to prevent her from becoming merely “Mana returned.”

Sakura’s character arc insists:

> **Inheritance is not replacement.**

She may carry something from Mana, but she is not Mana. She has her own body, appetite, family memories, local attachments, habits, friendships, weaknesses, and desires. The more the story risks turning her into a vessel, the more important her ordinary traits become.

Her story role also anchors SUNNY PEACE’s central contradiction. She is the unit’s sun, but the unit must not depend on her as the only source of light. Later material makes this sharper: if Sakura alone becomes “the sun,” her desire to keep shining can become self-destructive. SUNNY PEACE must learn that the sun is not Sakura alone; the five members must share the burden of brightness.

This makes Sakura both inspiring and dangerous. She gives the group its emotional ignition, but the group must love her enough not to let her burn herself out.

## 5\. Visual appearance

Sakura’s visual design is built around **sunlit approachability**.

Her hair is warm honey-blonde to light brown, usually soft, fluffy, and mobile. It is styled in ways that keep her silhouette buoyant and youthful. Her hair does not create a severe or elegant line like Rei’s; it creates warmth and motion.

Her eyes are large, amber-brown to reddish-brown, and very expressive. They are almost always emotionally legible. Sakura’s gaze invites. It does not hide.

Her silhouette is compact, rounded, and energetic. She often wears puffy sleeves, ribbons, short idol skirts, floral ornaments, food-themed or festival-themed costumes, and bright accessories. She is visually constructed as a burst of warmth at the center of the image.

Her color language centers on sunrise and festival colors: orange, yellow, coral, pink, white, sky blue, warm gold, and red accents. She looks natural among flowers, balloons, food, confetti, mascots, fireworks, and stage light.

Her body language is overwhelmingly open. She reaches toward the viewer, points outward, leans forward, sings with arms extended, laughs with her mouth open, and pulls people into the scene. She is not visually guarded.

The major motifs of Sakura’s design are:

-   sun;

-   flowers;

-   food;

-   festivals;

-   local life;

-   mascots;

-   reaching hands;

-   open smiles;

-   embodied joy.


Her design thesis is:

> **This girl is not a shrine to someone else’s miracle. She is alive.**

## 6\. Comparative formulation

Sakura’s member statement is:

> **I am alive, and I want to share this joy with you.**

Within SUNNY PEACE, she is the sun — but the best reading is not that she alone shines. It is that her life-affirming brightness teaches the others how to shine, and they in turn must protect her from becoming the sole bearer of light.

---

# III. Ichinose Rei

## 1\. Core thesis

**Ichinose Rei is SUNNY PEACE’s disciplined grace: a former competitive dancer whose arc transforms performance from proof into joy.**

Rei is often legible at first as the serious dancer, the elegant one, or the strict senpai-like member. Those readings are not wrong, but they are incomplete. Her deeper character lies in the tension between bodily discipline and emotional softness.

Rei learned to understand her body as something that must perform correctly, win, and satisfy expectations. Idolhood teaches her that movement can also be shared happiness, fan encouragement, gratitude, and self-expression.

Her core movement is:

> **from controlled proof to shared joy.**

Rei does not become less disciplined. She learns that discipline can serve tenderness.

## 2\. Personality

Rei is serious, diligent, physically aware, polite, responsible, and somewhat self-conscious. She often gives the impression of someone who is trying to do things properly. Compared with Sakura’s emotional immediacy, Rei’s personality is more measured. She thinks about form, training, standards, and whether she is fulfilling her role correctly.

Her core traits include:

First, **discipline**. Rei understands practice, correction, bodily control, and the value of repeated effort. She is not casual about performance. She respects the stage as a place where preparation matters.

Second, **earnestness**. Rei is not cynical or performatively detached. Her seriousness comes from caring too much, not too little. She wants to meet expectations and can become embarrassed when she feels she has failed to inhabit a role naturally.

Third, **latent softness**. Underneath the dancer’s composure is a girl who can be awkward, bashful, vulnerable, and surprisingly tender. She is not cold; she is controlled.

Fourth, **growth through relaxation**. Rei’s development often involves learning to smile naturally, accept praise, rest, rely on others, and enjoy being seen. Her greatest challenge is not effort. It is allowing effort to become pleasure.

## 3\. Voice and speech style

Rei’s voice is polite, composed, and serious, with a tendency toward careful phrasing. She sounds like someone used to respecting structure and responsibility. She is less impulsive than Sakura, less domestic and gently teasing than Haruko, less timid than Chisa, and less fanlike/dreamy than Shizuku.

Her speech often has the quality of someone who is trying to understand the correct emotional or social response. When she is in familiar territory — dance, practice, performance, improvement — she can sound confident. When placed in softer, romantic, or socially playful situations, she becomes more flustered.

Her vocal identity can be summarized as:

> **composure learning warmth.**

She is not verbally cold. Rather, her emotional warmth often comes through after a layer of discipline, hesitation, or self-consciousness.

## 4\. Characterization and story role

Rei gives SUNNY PEACE its physical and technical credibility. She is the member who makes the group’s warmth feel stageworthy. Sakura supplies emotional ignition; Rei gives that ignition form.

Her story is also one of the clearest examples of *IDOLY PRIDE*’s belief that idolhood transforms prior pain rather than erasing it. Rei’s dancer past is not discarded. The body that once existed to prove itself becomes a body that can encourage others.

Rei’s arc matters because she represents the redemption of training. In many idol stories, technical discipline can be framed as coldness or as something opposed to emotional sincerity. Rei complicates that. Her technique is real, valuable, and beautiful. The problem is not that she was disciplined. The problem is that discipline became tied to recognition, pressure, and fear of inadequacy.

SUNNY PEACE teaches her:

> You do not have to stop being excellent.
> You have to let excellence carry joy.

She also functions as a stabilizer within the group. Her physical competence and seriousness help balance Sakura’s impulsive brightness and the softer vulnerabilities of Chisa and Shizuku. Rei is not the emotional hearth — that is closer to Haruko — but she is the line, posture, and structure that let the hearth become performance.

## 5\. Visual appearance

Rei’s visual design is built around **elegant athleticism**.

Her most striking feature is her long pale-blonde hair, often flowing behind her or arranged with braids, ribbons, or refined ornaments. Her hair creates an elongated, graceful silhouette. It often acts as an extension of movement.

Her eyes are green or teal, calmer and sharper than Sakura’s. They often communicate focus, poise, or composed vulnerability.

Her silhouette is tall, long-limbed, and dancer-coded. She is frequently posed with controlled hands, extended legs, balanced posture, and elegant body lines. Even when seated or relaxed, her body tends to create a shaped composition.

Her color language centers on blue, white, aqua, gold, navy, cream, and occasionally black or red. Blue and white give her polish and cool elegance. Gold and blonde hair warm the image from within. Darker costumes reveal a more mature, glamorous, or severe Rei.

Her visual motifs include:

-   dance lines;

-   flowing hair;

-   blue-white stage costumes;

-   water and sky;

-   sunflowers;

-   elegant dresses;

-   practice wear;

-   controlled hands;

-   soft smiles emerging from composure.


The sunflower imagery is especially important. Sunflowers belong naturally to SUNNY PEACE’s sunny language, but Rei among sunflowers suggests that she has internalized the unit’s warmth. The disciplined dancer has learned to bloom.

Her design thesis is:

> **The body that once existed to prove itself can become a body that dances for others and smiles with friends.**

## 6\. Comparative formulation

Rei’s member statement is:

> **I have trained myself into form, and now I am learning to let that form carry joy.**

Within SUNNY PEACE, Rei is disciplined grace. Without her, the unit’s warmth risks becoming emotionally sincere but physically under-formed. Rei makes SUNNY PEACE capable.

---

# IV. Saeki Haruko

## 1\. Core thesis

**Saeki Haruko is SUNNY PEACE’s delayed-blooming warmth: a soft, mature, emotionally generous idol whose kindness becomes radiance, but who also longs to be seen as cute, special, romantic, and chosen.**

Haruko is often readable as the older-sister member, but that is only the surface. Her deeper characterization lies in the tension between **being the one who supports others** and **wanting to bloom herself**.

She is not simply mature. She is someone who worries that maturity might mean lateness: late to youth, late to romance, late to idolhood, late to being chosen first. SUNNY PEACE allows her to convert that fear into a different answer:

> **A girl who blooms later is still blooming.**

## 2\. Personality

Haruko is warm, kind, socially aware, nurturing, and emotionally steady. She is the member most naturally associated with comfort, hospitality, and interpersonal care. She often notices other people’s moods and tries to create a softer atmosphere.

Her core traits include:

First, **care**. Haruko has a strong instinct to support, feed, comfort, celebrate, and gently guide others. She makes emotional spaces livable.

Second, **self-conscious maturity**. She is aware of age, timing, ordinary adulthood, job hunting, marriage, romance, and the fear of being left behind. This gives her a more adult emotional texture than some of the other members.

Third, **delayed desire**. Haruko wants to be cute, glamorous, special, and personally cherished. She does not want to be reduced to a reliable caretaker. Her longing is soft but real.

Fourth, **persistence without bitterness**. Haruko’s idolhood is not based on being chosen early. It is based on continuing even after watching others shine first. This makes her warmth morally significant: she could have become resentful, but instead becomes generous.

## 3\. Voice and speech style

Haruko’s voice is gentle, warm, and socially smoothing. She often sounds like someone trying to make the other person comfortable. Her speech likely carries an older-sister softness: reassuring, mildly teasing, emotionally attentive, and careful.

But her voice also has moments of bashfulness and longing. When her own desires come into focus — wanting to be cute, wanting to be seen, wanting romance, wanting to be someone’s first choice — she can become more vulnerable.

Her vocal identity can be summarized as:

> **gentle care with delayed confession underneath.**

She is good at speaking for others’ comfort, but her most revealing moments are when she speaks from her own need.

## 4\. Characterization and story role

Haruko gives SUNNY PEACE its hearth. If Sakura is sunlight, Haruko is lamplight: less explosive, more inhabitable.

Her story role is crucial because SUNNY PEACE cannot be only youthful brightness. Haruko adds time, memory, patience, and adult tenderness. She represents the person who did not bloom immediately but still has beauty to offer.

Her presence reframes idolhood away from youthful destiny and toward cultivated warmth. She is not the prodigy, the miracle successor, the genius dancer, or the tiny anxious girl. She is the person who keeps going, who makes the table ready, who creates a room people want to return to.

This makes her essential to the group’s emotional ecology. Haruko stabilizes the others without flattening them. She can support Sakura’s brightness, help Rei relax, reassure Chisa, and share quiet warmth with Shizuku.

But her story also resists making support her whole identity. Haruko’s desire to be personally chosen matters. She does not want to be merely useful. Her idolhood asks whether kindness itself can become a visible, desirable form of light.

## 5\. Visual appearance

Haruko’s visual design is built around **soft maturity and delayed blooming**.

Her hair is dark brown, shoulder-length, gently wavy or softly curled. It feels natural and touchable rather than graphically iconic. Her eyes are warm amber or golden-brown, often soft, slightly drooping, and reassuring.

Her silhouette is rounded, feminine, and gentle. She is less associated with speed than Sakura and less associated with line than Rei. Her body language often emphasizes leaning, sitting, offering, hosting, reaching gently, and occupying shared spaces.

Her color language centers on warm earth tones, creams, greens, yellows, floral whites, muted blues, pinks, and romantic seasonal lighting. She looks especially natural in gardens, cafés, kitchens, parties, warm interiors, and flower-filled spaces.

Her visual motifs include:

-   cafés;

-   food;

-   flowers;

-   soft dresses;

-   aprons or domestic cues;

-   birthday cakes;

-   seasonal celebrations;

-   Christmas, Valentine, wedding, and pajama imagery;

-   warm indoor light;

-   gentle blushes;

-   mature but cute styling.


Haruko’s flower imagery is different from Sakura’s. Sakura’s flowers burst outward as celebration and life. Haruko’s flowers feel tended, cultivated, and waited for. They represent delayed blooming.

Her design thesis is:

> **Kindness can be radiance, ordinary warmth can become idol light, and a girl who thought she bloomed too late can still become someone’s most beautiful flower.**

## 6\. Comparative formulation

Haruko’s member statement is:

> **I have been waiting to bloom, and I want the warmth I give others to become my own light too.**

Within SUNNY PEACE, Haruko is hearth and flower. Without her, the unit’s cheerfulness would be brighter but less emotionally habitable.

---

# V. Shiraishi Chisa

## 1\. Core thesis

**Shiraishi Chisa is SUNNY PEACE’s handmade courage: a timid, soft, horror-loving, craft-oriented idol whose fear becomes empathy and whose visible effort helps others keep trying.**

Chisa is not simply the shy little-sister figure. She is one of SUNNY PEACE’s clearest embodiments of effort as idol value. Her power is not command, overwhelming talent, mature stability, or solar charisma. Her power is that fans can see her being scared and trying anyway.

Her core movement is:

> **from someone who needs support to someone whose need for support becomes the basis for supporting others.**

Chisa does not become compelling by ceasing to be timid. She becomes compelling by letting a timid girl stand onstage.

## 2\. Personality

Chisa is shy, gentle, anxious, earnest, affectionate, easily overwhelmed, and deeply effortful. She is sensitive to evaluation and often unsure of herself, but she has a stubborn capacity to continue.

Her personality contains several important strands.

First, **timidity**. Chisa often begins from fear or hesitation. She struggles with directness, visibility, and self-assertion.

Second, **effort**. Her defining virtue is not fearlessness but continuation. She does one difficult thing at a time: speaking, performing, asking for help, finishing a task, trying a new role, accepting attention.

Third, **empathy**. Because Chisa knows fear and inadequacy from the inside, she can stand beside others who feel the same. Her idolhood is based on 寄り添う力 / yorisou chikara — the power to stand beside or accompany someone.

Fourth, **craft and imagination**. Chisa is strongly associated with handmade objects, sewing, costumes, and turning inner feelings into small visible forms. She understands love through making things.

Fifth, **horror-cuteness**. Her interest in horror is not a contradiction to her timidity. It is how she plays with fear, aestheticizes it, and makes it shareable. Chisa does not overcome fear by denying it. She turns fear into experience, costume, story, and charm.

## 3\. Voice and speech style

Chisa’s voice is soft, hesitant, and emotionally transparent. She likely uses careful, deferential, or uncertain phrasing, especially when speaking to authority figures or when asked to assert herself. Her speech often carries the feeling of someone checking whether it is okay to say what she feels.

But Chisa’s voice becomes stronger when she talks about things she loves: her sister Saki, costumes, horror, handmade objects, friends, or fans. Her enthusiasm may still be shy, but it becomes more focused.

Her vocal identity can be summarized as:

> **trembling sincerity.**

She does not sound powerful because she dominates conversation. She sounds powerful when she says one honest thing despite fear.

## 4\. Characterization and story role

Chisa’s story role is to make courage small, visible, and repeatable.

Many idol stories emphasize grand breakthroughs: the big stage, the climactic confession, the dramatic center performance. Chisa’s development often works at a smaller scale. She grows through tiny acts of courage: speaking up, taking a step forward, accepting that others are watching, asking for help, finishing a costume, performing a role, cheering someone, or letting fans see her effort.

This makes her vital to SUNNY PEACE’s philosophy. The unit is not only for people who already shine. It is also for people who are scared of shining.

Chisa’s relationship to her sister Saki is central to her identity. Saki is her first image of radiance and protection, but Chisa’s arc requires her to become more than the younger sister chasing someone else’s light. She must learn to stand alone, then choose to stand with others as an equal.

Her role in SUNNY PEACE is not to be the weakest member. It is to make weakness communicable as strength. Fans can look at Chisa and think:

> If she can keep trying while scared, maybe I can too.

## 5\. Visual appearance

Chisa’s visual design is built around **smallness, softness, and fear becoming charm**.

Her hair is short, soft, light blonde or ash-blonde, with long bangs that often partially cover one eye. This asymmetry is one of her most important design features. She is visually half-hidden, half-emerging.

Her visible eye is large, purple, and vulnerable. It often looks anxious, sparkling, startled, or carefully hopeful.

Her silhouette is compact, rounded, and doll-like. Frills, ribbons, plushes, oversized accessories, soft textures, and small props all reinforce her petite presence.

Her color language centers on lavender, pink, pale yellow, mint, white, soft blue, and pastel green. These colors make her feel handmade, delicate, approachable, and emotionally safe. When horror or rock elements enter, black, red, purple, and neon accents appear, but they do not erase her softness.

Her visual motifs include:

-   frills;

-   ribbons;

-   plushes;

-   sewing and craft cues;

-   horror-fairy-tale imagery;

-   Alice-like costumes;

-   ghosts and gothic cuteness;

-   pajamas and comfort objects;

-   flowers;

-   maid/café imagery;

-   small microphones;

-   shy upward glances.


Her body language is often inward or object-mediated. She clutches things, holds props near herself, sits low, curls into the composition, hides part of her face, or looks upward as if negotiating with being seen.

Her design thesis is:

> **Idol radiance does not have to begin as confidence. It can begin as fear, effort, a handmade costume, a soft plush, a trembling voice, and the decision to stand where people can see you anyway.**

## 6\. Comparative formulation

Chisa’s member statement is:

> **I am scared, but I will keep trying — and maybe watching me try can help you keep trying too.**

Within SUNNY PEACE, Chisa is small visible courage. Without her, the unit’s warmth would be less accessible to fans who do not feel naturally bright or confident.

---

# VI. Hyōdō Shizuku

## 1\. Core thesis

**Hyōdō Shizuku is SUNNY PEACE’s fan-memory idol: a gentle, pastel, emotionally receptive girl whose idolhood returns the love she first received as a fan.**

Shizuku is not merely the quiet member. She is the member who most clearly bridges the audience and the stage. Her power comes from remembering what idol light feels like from below.

Her core movement is:

> **from observer to participant;
> from fan who treasures idols to idol who is treasured by fans;
> from someone who receives light to someone who returns it.**

Shizuku’s character is built around admiration, memory, collecting, noticing, and cherishing. She does not dominate space. She preserves its emotional meaning.

## 2\. Personality

Shizuku is soft, quiet, receptive, observant, affectionate, and inwardly enthusiastic. She is not loud, but she is not empty. Her emotional world is rich because she notices and treasures things.

Her major traits include:

First, **receptivity**. Shizuku absorbs atmosphere, emotion, and memory. She often seems to be moved by the world before she responds to it.

Second, **fanlike devotion**. Her love of idols is not abstract. She understands idol culture through goods, magazines, penlights, events, saved memories, and the feeling of looking up from the audience.

Third, **gentle enthusiasm**. Shizuku’s excitement may not be explosive like Sakura’s, but it is deep. She can become intensely invested in what she loves.

Fourth, **emotional interpretation**. She is good at understanding the meaning of feelings, memories, and fan experiences. She often feels like the member most able to translate between idol and audience.

Fifth, **quiet attachment**. Shizuku bonds through closeness, shared objects, shared memories, and being near someone. Her intimacy style is soft and companionable.

## 3\. Voice and speech style

Shizuku’s voice is gentle, dreamy, and emotionally receptive. She likely speaks softly and carefully, with a quality of wonder or quiet sincerity. Her speech can feel as though she is preserving the emotional weight of what she says.

Unlike Sakura, she does not close distance through energetic exclamation. Unlike Rei, she does not primarily speak from discipline. Unlike Haruko, she does not primarily speak as caretaker. Unlike Chisa, she is not defined by trembling effort. Shizuku speaks as someone who has looked carefully, loved quietly, and wants to express what that love means.

Her vocal identity can be summarized as:

> **quiet admiration becoming expression.**

She sounds most like herself when articulating what something meant to her.

## 4\. Characterization and story role

Shizuku’s story role is to bring fandom onto the stage without betraying fandom.

Many idol works treat the fan and idol positions as separate: the fan looks up; the idol shines. Shizuku complicates that division. She begins from the audience side, from admiration and memory. Her idolhood is not a rejection of that origin. It is its continuation.

Her character says:

> The person holding the penlight can become the person standing under the penlights.

This makes her indispensable to SUNNY PEACE’s emotional philosophy. SUNNY PEACE is a unit built on receiving and returning feeling, and Shizuku understands that process from the fan side. She knows that an idol moment can become someone’s memory, comfort, courage, or treasured object.

Her role is therefore less explosive than Sakura’s, less structural than Rei’s, less hearthlike than Haruko’s, and less effort-visible than Chisa’s — but it is equally important. Shizuku protects the meaning of idol light as something received.

She is the member who reminds SUNNY PEACE that idols are not only performers. They are also memories in someone else’s life.

## 5\. Visual appearance

Shizuku’s visual design is built around **soft receptivity and fan-goods dreaminess**.

Her hair is pale aqua-blue, often styled in twin buns, side tails, ribbons, or curled sections. It creates a fluffy, rounded, cloudlike silhouette. Her eyes are pinkish-red to purple-red, large, soft, and often slightly overwhelmed by wonder.

Her silhouette is petite, rounded, and decorative. She is often surrounded by ribbons, plushes, pastel objects, magazines, cards, penlights, mascots, and fan-adjacent goods.

Her color language centers on aqua, mint, lavender, pink, white, pale yellow, and candy-like accents. These colors make her feel like idol merchandise, pastel stationery, acrylic stands, plush packaging, magazine pages, and fan-event decoration.

Her visual motifs include:

-   magazines;

-   glowsticks;

-   idol goods;

-   plushes;

-   mascots;

-   rabbits or bears;

-   glasses;

-   cards and hobby-room objects;

-   angelic white-blue costumes;

-   dreamy stage light;

-   soft seated poses;

-   emotional upward gazes.


Her body language is usually gentle and low-intensity. She sits, leans, holds objects close, looks upward or sideways, and appears caught in moments of wonder. Even when performing, she often feels emotionally receptive rather than commanding.

Her design thesis is:

> **Idolhood is not only the act of shining. It is remembering what it felt like to be reached by someone else’s light, then stepping onto the stage so that feeling can be returned.**

## 6\. Comparative formulation

Shizuku’s member statement is:

> **I loved idols first, and now I want to return that love from the stage.**

Within SUNNY PEACE, Shizuku is fan-memory. Without her, the unit loses the audience-side emotional intelligence that makes its warmth reciprocal.

---

# VII. SUNNY PEACE internal dynamics

## 1\. Sakura and Rei

Sakura and Rei form a useful contrast between emotional immediacy and disciplined form.

Sakura moves outward first. Rei shapes movement. Sakura’s energy says “come with me”; Rei’s discipline says “let’s make this beautiful enough to reach.” Sakura helps Rei relax into joy. Rei helps Sakura’s radiance become stable performance.

Their dynamic is not opposition but completion:

> Sakura gives Rei warmth.
> Rei gives Sakura form.

## 2\. Sakura and Haruko

Sakura and Haruko are two different kinds of warmth.

Sakura is sunlight: immediate, public, life-affirming, forward-moving. Haruko is hearth: gentle, domestic, mature, patient, and inhabitable.

Sakura’s joy can be urgent. Haruko’s warmth can slow the room down enough for people to breathe. Haruko is one of the members best suited to notice when Sakura’s brightness is becoming burden. Sakura, in turn, helps Haruko feel that blooming is still possible.

## 3\. Sakura and Chisa

Sakura and Chisa embody two opposite routes to encouragement.

Sakura encourages through abundance: “life is wonderful; let’s go.” Chisa encourages through effort: “I am scared too, but let’s try.” Sakura’s energy can pull Chisa forward, but Chisa’s vulnerability reminds Sakura that not everyone can run at the same speed.

Their combined message to fans is powerful:

> Joy matters.
> So does fear.
> You can move with either.

## 4\. Sakura and Shizuku

Sakura is the idol who makes ordinary life glow. Shizuku is the fan who knows what it means to treasure that glow. Sakura naturally generates moments; Shizuku preserves their emotional meaning.

Sakura’s warmth becomes memory through Shizuku’s gaze. Shizuku’s admiration becomes performance through Sakura’s example.

## 5\. Rei and Haruko

Rei and Haruko contrast disciplined elegance with soft maturity.

Rei’s issue is learning to relax into joy. Haruko’s issue is learning that her warmth can be her own light, not just support for others. Haruko can soften Rei’s severity; Rei can help Haruko feel that her stage presence has form and legitimacy.

Together, they give SUNNY PEACE a more mature register. They prevent the unit from becoming only genki, cute, or childlike.

## 6\. Rei and Chisa

Rei and Chisa are both effort-based characters, but their effort feels different.

Rei’s effort is disciplined, physical, technical, and performance-oriented. Chisa’s effort is emotional, social, and courage-oriented. Rei learns that technical perfection must carry feeling. Chisa learns that fear does not disqualify performance.

Rei can model practice for Chisa. Chisa can remind Rei that vulnerability is also communicable.

## 7\. Rei and Shizuku

Rei and Shizuku pair elegance with admiration.

Rei is someone whose body has been trained to be watched. Shizuku is someone who understands what watching means. This makes their closeness especially meaningful: Shizuku can appreciate Rei’s beauty without reducing her to technique, while Rei can help Shizuku step more confidently from observer into performer.

## 8\. Haruko and Chisa

Haruko and Chisa share domestic softness, but at different life stages.

Haruko cares with mature warmth. Chisa seeks comfort through handmade, plush, and safe-room imagery. Haruko can provide a reassuring older-sister atmosphere without trapping Chisa in dependency. Chisa, in turn, reflects Haruko’s own desire to be allowed softness rather than always performing maturity.

## 9\. Haruko and Shizuku

Haruko and Shizuku are both memory-oriented characters.

Haruko carries delayed dreams and the feeling of having waited to bloom. Shizuku carries fan memories and the feeling of having looked up from below. Both understand that idolhood is not only a present-tense performance; it is something that accumulates in people.

Together, they give SUNNY PEACE depth of time.

## 10\. Chisa and Shizuku

Chisa and Shizuku are the two softest visual presences in the unit, but they differ sharply.

Chisa’s softness is fear becoming courage. Shizuku’s softness is admiration becoming expression. Chisa is the timid girl learning to be seen. Shizuku is the fan learning to be watched.

Together, they represent SUNNY PEACE’s gentlest axis:

> The scared person can stand on stage.
> The person in the audience can become the idol.
> The small feeling can become public light.
